### 11.38 Cookie-only browser access sessions

The SPA no longer receives, persists, reads, or sends an access JWT. Login and refresh response contracts contain only the session expiry and authorized profile/capability projection; both access and refresh secrets are issued as `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`, essential cookies. The short-lived access cookie is scoped to `/api`, while the refresh cookie is scoped to `/api/auth`. Production cookies require HTTPS. Cookie deletion now repeats the original path and security policy, so logout, current/all-session revocation, password change, and MFA state changes reliably expire both values rather than attempting to delete a differently scoped cookie.

`JwtBearerEvents.OnMessageReceived` accepts the access cookie for browser calls. An explicit Bearer header retains precedence, preserving Swagger and non-browser API compatibility without creating a second authorization model. Refresh no longer accepts an expired access token in its request body: it resolves the user and session family from the hashed refresh-cookie value, applies the existing account, revocation, idle, absolute-expiry, and security-stamp rules, then rotates the family transactionally. The browser sends credentialed requests and retains only a non-secret tab marker for routing. Concurrent 401 responses share one in-flight refresh operation, preventing avoidable same-tab rotation races. Legacy `auth_token` storage is removed on module load and during every authentication transition.

Backend regression tests prove that production cookie policy is HttpOnly, Secure, Strict, essential, and correctly path-scoped, and that the login projection cannot serialize access or refresh token properties. Frontend tests prove that login stores no token, protected calls send no Bearer header, refresh sends no token body, credential cookies are included, and simultaneous failures coalesce to one rotation. Backend **116/116** tests pass with one SQL Server integration test explicitly skipped; frontend **98/98** tests pass across **26** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, host/frontend production builds, and `git diff --check` pass. No schema migration or provider-specific persistence code was required, so SQLite interim verification and SQL Server deployment use the same application implementation; the idempotent SQL Server migration script remains **271,968 bytes**.

Session/token storage is repository-complete for the current local-identity architecture. At that increment, authentication remained **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending password-reset delivery, compromised-password screening, configurable Entra ID/Active Directory/hybrid providers, production distributed-session rehearsal, and browser penetration/CSRF acceptance. Section 11.39 closes the password-reset repository gap.

### 11.39 Enumeration-safe one-time password reset

The public password-reset request and completion contracts are implemented at `/api/v1/auth/password/forgot` and `/api/v1/auth/password/reset` under the existing authentication rate limit. Request responses are deliberately identical for missing, inactive, unconfirmed, and valid accounts. Identity generates a bounded-lifetime, single-use data-protection token only for an eligible user. `PasswordResetNotifier` delivers the percent-encoded link directly through the configured email adapter with a non-secret idempotency key. Email and token are carried in the link fragment, which browsers do not send to the web server or access logs; the token is never placed in the workflow outbox, application database, audit values, or API response. Production rejects a non-HTTPS reset origin, and the configured token lifetime is also applied to Identity's data-protection token provider.

Successful reset executes inside a relational transaction, applies the same Identity password validators as registration/change, clears `MustChangePassword`, rotates the security stamp, revokes every governed session family, clears any browser session cookies, and appends `PasswordResetCompleted` authentication audit evidence without the password or token. Invalid, expired, and already-consumed links return a common error. A request audit stores only delivery success/provider metadata. The unauthenticated login surface now provides request and reset forms, confirmation matching, policy guidance, generic account-discovery text, server validation messages, and safe return to sign-in. Email, token, and password travel only in POST bodies; no secret is added to an API URL.

SQLite controller integration proves equal known/unknown responses, eligible-user delivery, token-free request audit, real Identity password replacement, forced-change clearing, security-stamp rotation, governed-session revocation, and completion audit. Delivery tests prove exact encoding for tokens containing reserved characters and production HTTPS enforcement. Frontend tests prove the generic request flow, link consumption, success return, and versioned POST transport. Backend **119/119** tests pass with one SQL Server integration test explicitly skipped; frontend **101/101** tests pass across **26** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, backend/frontend production builds, and `git diff --check` pass. No schema migration or provider-specific application code was introduced; the SQL Server migration script remains **271,968 bytes**.

Password-reset delivery is repository-complete and requires only the already-documented production email provider provisioning. At that increment, authentication remained **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending compromised-password screening, configurable Entra ID/Active Directory/hybrid providers, authentication-policy administration, production distributed-session rehearsal, and browser penetration/CSRF acceptance. Section 11.40 closes the compromised-password repository gap.

### 11.40 Compromised-password screening

`CompromisedPasswordValidator` is registered in the ASP.NET Core Identity validator pipeline, so administrator-created accounts, self-service password change, forced password remediation, seed-controlled password replacement, and one-time password reset cannot bypass the same rule. A deterministic local denylist always rejects common/default OPMS and administrative passwords, including when external screening is intentionally disabled for offline development. The login and settings surfaces state that common or breached values are rejected rather than presenting complexity alone as sufficient security.

Production enables a fail-closed `PwnedPasswordLookup`. It computes SHA-1 locally, sends only the first five hexadecimal characters to the configured HTTPS range endpoint, requests response padding, supplies the provider-required user agent, bounds the response size and timeout, ignores zero-count padding entries, and compares the remaining suffix locally. Neither the password nor its complete hash leaves the application. Successful prefix ranges are cached briefly without password/user association. The implementation follows the provider's documented [Pwned Passwords k-anonymity range protocol](https://haveibeenpwned.com/API/v3). Any provider failure blocks password creation/change/reset with a safe retry message in production. `CompromisedPasswordHealthCheck` also makes readiness unhealthy when fail-closed screening cannot reach the range service and reports degraded configuration if screening is enabled without fail-closed behavior.

Automated tests prove that only the five-character prefix is transmitted, the full hash and password are absent from the request, padding and user-agent headers are present, padded rows are ignored, occurrence thresholds are enforced, prefix results are cached, local common values are rejected without a network call, remote breach matches are rejected, fail-closed outages reject password writes, disabled development screening retains local protection, and production readiness fails on provider outage. Backend **123/123** tests pass with one SQL Server integration test explicitly skipped; frontend **101/101** tests pass across **26** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, backend/frontend production builds, and `git diff --check` pass. No schema migration or database-provider-specific application code was introduced; the SQL Server migration script remains **271,968 bytes**.

Compromised-password screening is repository-complete for local Identity. Authentication remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending configurable Entra ID/Active Directory/hybrid providers, administrator-facing authentication-policy governance, production distributed-session rehearsal, and browser penetration/CSRF acceptance.

### 11.41 Governed IDP plan and version lineage

IDP plans now carry both a record-level `PublicId` and a stable `PlanFamilyId`. A successor can reference one same-tenant predecessor and inherits its family identifier; independent plans start a new family. Plans also carry effective-from/effective-to dates, publication timestamp/reference, and RowVersion. The controller rejects reversed financial or effective periods, missing/cross-tenant predecessors, and successors that predate their predecessor. Transitioning a plan to Published requires an explicit publication reference and records the publication timestamp.

Every IDP plan version now has its own public ID, optional predecessor-version link, effective period, publication evidence, and RowVersion. Creating a version links it to the active predecessor, requires a later effective date within the parent plan's effective period, closes the predecessor's effective period, and leaves only the successor active. Hierarchy and plan-list responses expose the lineage without leaking internal foreign keys. The dashboard no longer performs synthetic plan or annual-review writes; it routes authorized users to a governed workspace. That workspace captures real plan identity, predecessor, effective dates, publication reference, version type/label/review year/change summary, and version effective/publication metadata.

Migration `20261002102623_V39IdpPlanLineage` first adds nullable legacy-bridge columns, deterministically backfills every existing plan family and version public ID, derives predecessor-version links by version order, closes inactive effective periods, and only then makes required columns non-null and creates indexes, checks, and restricted self-references. SQL Server keeps native generated `rowversion`; the shared EF model advances concurrency values for SQLite verification. No controller/domain code changes are required when the deployment connection remains SQL Server.

Focused backend tests pass **13/13**, including SQLite relational enforcement of version-public-ID uniqueness and effective-period checks, plan-family continuation, predecessor chaining, active-period closure, and publication rules. Frontend tests pass **2/2** for governed navigation and exact lineage payload transport. The complete suites pass **126/126** backend tests with one explicitly skipped SQL Server acceptance test and **103/103** frontend tests across **27** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, host/frontend builds, model/snapshot consistency, `git diff --check`, and generation of a **281,338-byte** idempotent SQL Server migration script pass. R-48 is repository-complete; native SQL Server migration/`rowversion` execution remains an environment acceptance item, while optional IDP staging/import/reconciliation remains separately open under R-49.

### 11.42 Configuration-only SQLite development and SQL Server deployment

The host no longer hard-codes its EF provider in application startup. `Database:Provider` selects `Sqlite` or `SqlServer`, and `ConnectionStrings:DefaultConnection` supplies the corresponding connection. The default and production settings remain `SqlServer`; a local environment or User Secrets override can select SQLite without recompiling or changing domain, API, service, or persistence code. `.env.example` documents both modes without embedding credentials, and local SQLite database, shared-memory, and write-ahead-log files are ignored by Git.

Disposable local schema creation is an explicit `Database:EnsureCreated` switch. Startup rejects that switch outside Development and rejects it for SQL Server, preserving controlled EF migrations as the only deployment path. SQL Server remains the authoritative production model and continues to use native generated `rowversion`; SQLite uses the existing portable concurrency-token advancement solely for local relational verification. The shared model, query filters, constraints, and write guards are unchanged.

Five focused tests prove SQL Server and SQLite selection, reject unknown providers, require a SQLite connection string, and create the complete shared schema containing IDP, security, and OPMS tables through the configured SQLite path. The full backend suite passes **131/131** with the one explicit SQL Server acceptance test still skipped. Release build, model/snapshot consistency, the unchanged **281,338-byte** idempotent SQL Server migration script, and an online transitive NuGet advisory scan all pass; the scan reports no vulnerable packages. Deployment back to SQL Server is therefore configuration-only, but native SQL Server migration/constraint/`rowversion` execution remains mandatory before release.

### 11.43 Governed IDP KPI import and reconciliation

The optional import path now stages KPI definitions beneath an existing tenant-owned IDP plan through versioned `/api/v1/idp` endpoints. A client request ID and canonical server-side SHA-256 make staging idempotent. Each immutable staged row records the source payload, normalized candidate, current value, classification, validation detail, and expected KPI RowVersion. Reconciliation classifies every row as NEW, UNCHANGED, CHANGED, or INVALID and prevents commit until the entire batch is valid. Source-row uniqueness, count consistency, public identifiers, tenant ownership, and append-only evidence are also database-enforced.

Commit requires the original batch RowVersion and a reason, re-runs reconciliation against current records, rejects stale classifications or target versions, and applies every NEW/CHANGED row in one transaction. Stage and commit write audit evidence. Both browser capability checks and direct endpoints require the stable `IDP_INDICATOR.IMPORT` action, while tenant query filters prevent cross-municipality batch or row access. Manual and imported KPI creation use `IdpKpiDefinitionPolicy`, preventing validation drift; the workspace provides a downloadable CSV template, robust quoted-field parsing, reconciliation counts/details, commit controls, and recent batch history.

Seven focused backend tests cover all four classifications, invalid-batch rejection, idempotent staging, atomic audited commit, stale-preview rollback, append-only evidence, count and source-row uniqueness constraints, tenant isolation, concurrency-token validation, and the shared manual/import policy. CSV and workspace/transport tests cover quoted input, typed validation, capability gating, idempotency, RowVersion, preview, and commit. The complete suites pass **137/137** backend tests with one native SQL Server acceptance test skipped and **108/108** frontend tests across **28** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, host/frontend builds, model/snapshot consistency, `git diff --check`, and a **288,069-byte** idempotent SQL Server migration script pass.

This increment closed the governed KPI-definition slice. The full-hierarchy/project pipeline added in section 11.45 subsequently closes the remaining repository-side R-49 gap. SQLite proves provider-neutral relational behavior; native SQL Server migration, constraints, foreign keys, and generated `rowversion` behavior remain mandatory deployment acceptance checks and require no domain, API, service, or UI code change.

### 11.44 Governed withdrawal and no-hard-delete lifecycle

OPMS and IPMS targets and submissions now use an explicit withdrawal action rather than deletion. Each versioned endpoint requires a bounded governance reason and current RowVersion, evaluates the stable `OPMS_KPI.WITHDRAW`, `IPMS_KPI.WITHDRAW`, `OPMS_SUBMISSION.WITHDRAW`, or `IPMS_SUBMISSION.WITHDRAW` action against the record scope, and operates only inside the selected municipality. The target or submission is retained with actor/time/reason metadata while a separate `GovernedRecordLifecycleEvent` records the tenant, aggregate, action, actor, timestamp, reason and correlation identifier. The ledger has restricted foreign keys, a tenant query filter, and DbContext-level update/delete rejection.

Withdrawn targets cannot be edited or used for new submissions. Withdrawn submissions cannot be edited, scored, advanced through workflow, extended, uploaded to, or have evidence replaced; preservation operations such as authorized download, malware rescan, assessment, legal hold and governed retention disposal remain available where their independent policies permit them. Target and submission DELETE routes return 410 with the governed replacement contract. POE DELETE routes continue to reject destructive removal, their browser transport and controls were removed, and security permission registry entries can only be deactivated rather than physically deleted. The SPA replaces trash/delete actions with capability-gated withdrawal dialogs that require a reason and transport the current concurrency token while leaving the retained record visible.

Migration `20261002112405_V39GovernedRecordWithdrawalLifecycle` adds withdrawal actor/time/reason metadata, creates the lifecycle ledger, reconciles legacy withdrawn/disabled rows, and only then installs database checks requiring complete lifecycle metadata. Its data-reconciliation SQL and constraint expressions are valid for the SQL Server deployment model and the interim SQLite relational model; provider choice remains configuration-only. Three focused backend tests prove successful retained withdrawal plus audit invocation, stale-token rollback without lifecycle evidence, database metadata constraints, append-only enforcement and cross-tenant filtering. Frontend coverage proves mandatory normalized reason capture and the versioned withdrawal route/payload. The complete suites pass **141/141** backend tests with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped and **110/110** frontend tests across **29** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, Debug and Release host/frontend builds, model/snapshot consistency, `git diff --check`, and generation of a **297,141-byte** idempotent SQL Server migration script pass.

R-39 is now **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Native SQL Server application of the migration, checks, restricted foreign keys and generated `rowversion` concurrency remains a mandatory deployment acceptance check, but switching from SQLite requires configuration only and no domain, controller, service or UI code change.

### 11.45 Governed full-hierarchy IDP import and reconciliation

The governed IDP import pipeline now accepts a complete denormalized hierarchy/project source row: strategic outcome, objective, development priority, programme and project, including effective dates, budgets, responsible-department codes, project status and community-need reference. Codes are normalized and validated, dates must fit the selected tenant-owned plan, department references must resolve inside that municipality, and duplicate project paths or inconsistent definitions of a shared parent are rejected before any governed hierarchy record is written. Multiple project rows can intentionally share one identical parent chain without duplicating it.

Staging uses the same immutable, idempotent batch evidence model as KPI reconciliation. Each row retains the original payload, normalized hierarchy snapshot, current hierarchy snapshot, validation result and a combined digest of every existing record RowVersion in its path. Commit re-runs the complete reconciliation and rejects any changed classification, snapshot or concurrency digest, then applies all NEW/CHANGED hierarchy definitions in one transaction. Hierarchy stage and commit have dedicated versioned routes protected by `IDP_PLAN.IMPORT`; the KPI commit route rejects hierarchy batches, preserving direct-API authorization when browser controls are hidden. Both operations write reasoned audit evidence. The SPA selects hierarchy/project or KPI mode from dynamic capabilities, provides a complete hierarchy CSV template and typed parser, displays the shared reconciliation preview, and dispatches to the correctly authorized commit route.

Outcome, objective, priority, programme and project records now have stable unique `PublicId` values and RowVersion concurrency tokens. Development priorities also have a stable objective-scoped code. Migration `20261002114215_V39IdpHierarchyImport` adds nullable bridge columns, provider-aware legacy backfill, then required columns and unique indexes; SQL Server uses `NEWID()` and native generated `rowversion`, while SQLite supports interim relational verification without application-code divergence. The generated SQL Server migration block was inspected for its backfill and unique priority-code index.

### 11.46 Independent Technical Indicator Description module

Each municipality now controls two explicit settings: `TidEnabled` and `TidAllKpisRequired`. Disabling the optional module leaves every OPMS target and submission path unchanged. Enabling the all-KPIs policy produces a scope-aware compliance count and highlights missing descriptions; it does not manufacture another KPI or block ordinary reporting workflow. Configuration changes require `TID.CONFIGURE`, a reason and the municipality RowVersion and are audit logged.

`TechnicalIndicatorDescription` is a tenant-owned version record linked to one existing OPMS target. It captures the indicator definition, purpose, data source, collection method, calculation method, paired numerator/denominator descriptions where applicable, limitations, assumptions, verification method, optional responsible municipal employee, notes, effective dates, author, predecessor and RowVersion. There is one database-enforced current version per KPI and a unique monotonically numbered lineage. A successor requires `TID.UPDATE` and the prior RowVersion, atomically closes only the previous current version, appends the successor and audit evidence, and preserves every prior methodology record. DbContext enforcement rejects deletion or arbitrary mutation of historical versions. TID never writes target, actual, calculation type, OPMS unit, required POE, submission or workflow responsibility.

`TidSourceDocument` is an append-only association from a TID version to the existing private `EvidenceBlob` store. Uploads are limited to approved document/image formats and 25 MB, validate file signatures, fail closed through malware scanning, remain quarantined until clean, and are downloaded only after a clean scan and a fresh `TID.READ` record-scope decision. Authorized `TID.UPLOAD_SOURCE` users can rescan quarantined content. Storage keys, hashes and scanner evidence remain server-side; upload and rescan actions are audited.

The versioned `/api/v1/tids` API applies the central dynamic-security engine to list, history, version, configuration, upload, rescan and download operations. List and compliance counts filter in SQL through the same department/unit/owner/assignment scope used by the linked KPI. Navigation `NAV.SDBIP.TIDS` is registered dynamically. The React workspace shows the municipality policy, scoped compliance counts, authorised KPI register, current methodology, complete version/source history, capability-gated version form, employee selector, source upload/rescan and private download. Read-only operational users receive history without administration controls.

Migration `20261002120350_V39TechnicalIndicatorDescriptions` adds the two municipality policy flags, version and source-document tables, restricted tenant/KPI/employee/user/blob/self-lineage foreign keys, public-ID and business-key uniqueness, effective-period checks, a filtered unique current-version index and native SQL Server RowVersion. Five focused backend tests prove policy concurrency/audit, mandatory-policy coverage, lineage and KPI independence, append-only enforcement, private quarantine/rescan/download, direct-write denial and tenant isolation. Frontend coverage proves policy/version workflows, read-only behavior, navigation gating, exact versioned routes and absence of operational KPI values from TID writes.

The complete suites pass **148/148** backend tests with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped and **118/118** frontend tests across **30** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, Debug and Release host/frontend builds, model/snapshot consistency, `git diff --check`, and generation of a **315,313-byte** idempotent SQL Server migration script pass. R-50 is **COMPLIANT** at repository level; native SQL Server execution and production file/scanner provisioning remain deployment acceptance items and require configuration rather than domain/API/UI code changes.

### 11.47 Governed Strategic Documents

Strategic documents are now managed as tenant-owned, financial-year-scoped document families rather than opaque uploaded filenames. Administrators can configure document types with stable codes, display order, activation state and an explicit external-link policy. Each document version records a stable family identifier, predecessor, monotonically increasing version, optional SDBIP layer, user-facing title, description, document date, display order, author and RowVersion. Content is exactly one private managed file or an approved HTTPS link; embedded credentials, cross-tenant references and disallowed link types are rejected. A successor preserves the prior version and closes only its current-version projection.

Approval, publication, retirement and malware-rescan are separate permission-protected commands. Publication requires prior approval and clean, active content. Every lifecycle command uses the selected municipality, centralized record scope, a current RowVersion, actor/time metadata, a mandatory reason where applicable, an append-only `StrategicDocumentEvent` and the normal audit service. Ordinary authorised readers receive only the latest active, approved and published version for each family in their selected municipality and financial year. A later draft does not hide the last published version. Direct content requests repeat the same authorization and visibility checks; storage keys, original filenames, hashes and scanner evidence stay server-side, while the UI consistently displays the governed title.

The versioned `/api/v1/strategic-documents` surface provides controlled type administration, scoped register/search, administrative family history, multipart initial/successor creation, approval, publication, retirement, rescan and protected content retrieval. Navigation `NAV.STRATEGIC_DOCUMENTS`, resource CRUD, and `MANAGE_TYPES`, `APPROVE`, `PUBLISH`, `RETIRE` and `RESCAN` actions are seeded into the dynamic security registry. The React workspace exposes financial-year/search filters, type maintenance, file/link version creation, lifecycle actions, private download/external-link access and ordered version/action history only when the effective capability permits them.

Migration `20261002122820_V39StrategicDocuments` creates the type, document and append-only event tables with restricted municipality, financial-year, type, predecessor, blob and actor foreign keys; tenant-scoped public/business-key uniqueness; one filtered current version per family; content-source, publication/approval, version and display-order checks; query filters; and native SQL Server RowVersion columns. DbContext guards reject event mutation, document deletion and arbitrary rewriting of historical versions. Four focused backend tests cover type audit/concurrency, external-link lineage, draft-successor visibility, file quarantine/rescan/approval/publication/download, tenant isolation and direct permission denial. Frontend coverage proves read-only and capability-gated workflows plus exact API routing.

The complete suites pass **152/152** backend tests with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped and **123/123** frontend tests across **31** files. Strategic Documents are **COMPLIANT** at repository level. ### 11.48 Independent Circular 88 subsystem

Circular 88 is now a separately bounded optional subsystem. `C88MunicipalityConfiguration` selects one published catalogue edition for a tenant-owned municipality financial year and can be disabled without changing any ordinary OPMS target, actual, TID, POE, submission or workflow record. Catalogue editions are assembled as drafts and become immutable when published. Publication is blocked until the edition contains sectors, outcomes, indicator types, municipal categories, readiness tiers, report types, response types, an indicator with data elements and applicability, and compliance questions. Official formula text remains descriptive; executable behavior is limited to the enumerated None, Sum, Average, Ratio, Percentage and Difference operators.

The catalogue models official indicators and technical indicator descriptions, typed data elements, applicability and typed compliance questions. Planning records validate required baseline, medium-term and annual values against the indicator value type, with an explicit missing-data explanation and estimated availability alternative. Reporting calendars bind report types to open/close/due windows and optional canonical reporting periods. Indicator reports preserve stable family/version lineage, data-element values, compliance responses, controlled calculated value, RowVersion and append-only workflow actions. A successor is the only way to revise report content; current-version uniqueness and DbContext guards prevent competing or rewritten report evidence.

Effective-dated `PRIMARY_CAPTURER`, `CONTRIBUTOR`, `REVIEWER_VERIFIER` and `FINAL_SUBMITTER` assignments are tenant/financial-year/indicator scoped, with one database-enforced active open-ended Primary Capturer. Separately versioned C88 workflows require Capturer, Reviewer/Verifier and Final Submission stages. Each report pins the exact workflow definition used at capture, so later configuration cannot change historical routing. Submit, verify, return and final-submit commands require both a dynamic action permission and the effective assignment, use RowVersion, append reasoned action/audit evidence and never invoke ordinary PMS or Internal Audit stages.

`C88OpmsMapping` provides `DIRECT` and `CONTRIBUTING` alignment only. The mapping stores references and audit evidence but contains no propagation path for OPMS target, actual, TID, POE or workflow values. Ordinary readers are filtered to indicators with an effective C88 assignment; administrative access, catalogue/configuration, assignments, workflow and mapping use distinct registered actions. `/api/v1/c88` supplies the scoped workspace plus explicit catalogue, configuration, planning, calendar, assignment, workflow, mapping, report-version and workflow-command endpoints. Five dynamic navigation items expose planning, reporting, compliance, mapping and reports through the common security engine, and the React workspace hides each mutation surface using the same effective capabilities while direct API checks remain authoritative.

Migration `20261002130455_V39Circular88` creates the 17 tenant-scoped catalogue, configuration, planning, reporting, assignment, workflow and mapping tables with restricted foreign keys, public/business-key uniqueness, effective/date/sequence/version checks, filtered current-version and Primary Capturer uniqueness, query filters and native SQL Server RowVersion columns. Five focused relational/controller tests prove edition completeness/immutability, controlled numeric and response-type validation, planning, report calculation, pinned workflow history, append-only evidence, tenant isolation, assignment-scoped reads, direct API denial, Primary Capturer uniqueness, alignment-only mappings and disabled-module independence.

The complete suites pass **157/157** backend tests with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped and **127/127** frontend tests across **33** files. Success and error notifications use appropriate polite/assertive live regions. These primitives improve every feature that composes them without introducing page-specific behavior or database-provider coupling.

### 11.50 Operational telemetry, readiness and response hardening

**Requirement:** R-52 / Phase 11 monitoring, health and security-test hardening.

**Implementation:** Every HTTP request now contributes to bounded `System.Diagnostics.Metrics` counters, an active-request gauge and duration histogram under meter `FTCERP.Host`, plus an `ActivitySource` named `FTCERP.Host.Operations`. Labels are deliberately limited to request method and response status class to avoid tenant, user, URL or record cardinality and disclosure. The existing validated correlation identifier is included in the structured logging scope and trace tag, while completion logs capture status and duration without request payloads or credentials. An in-process snapshot drives configurable failure-rate and average-duration readiness degradation after a minimum sample size. This supplies deterministic alert conditions without claiming that an external pager exists.

The readiness endpoint now returns stable machine-readable JSON containing only check name, status and duration; health descriptions, exceptions and data dictionaries are not exposed anonymously. Existing database, outbox, storage, malware-scanner, notification-channel and compromised-password checks remain part of readiness, while liveness intentionally avoids dependency probes. Default browser responses now include content-type, framing, referrer, feature-permission and restrictive content-security policies. Production thresholds are configuration, and the metrics/activity sources can be collected by standard .NET/OpenTelemetry deployment instrumentation without changing domain, controller, service, UI or database-provider code.

**Files/classes:** `Infrastructure/Observability/OperationalTelemetry.cs`, `Infrastructure/Health/OperationalTelemetryHealthCheck.cs`, `Infrastructure/Health/HealthResponseWriter.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/SecurityHeadersMiddleware.cs`, `Program.cs`, `appsettings.json`, `appsettings.Production.json`, and `.env.example`.

**Migration:** None. The feature is provider-neutral and SQL Server deployment remains a configuration/migration operation; SQLite can continue to exercise repository behavior temporarily.

**API:** `GET /health/live` remains a dependency-free process probe. `GET /health/ready` executes all dependency and telemetry checks and emits a non-sensitive JSON contract suitable for an orchestrator or external monitor.

**Tests:** `OperationalReadinessTests` supplies four focused tests for successful/failed request accounting, active-gauge cleanup, configured degradation, security headers and non-disclosing readiness JSON. The focused suite passes **4/4**. The complete backend suite passes **161/161** with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped; the complete frontend suite remains green at **129/129** across **34** files. Release host/frontend builds, TypeScript type-check and ESLint also pass.

**Status:** R-52 advances from **NOT IMPLEMENTED** to **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT**. Repository health, correlation, structured monitoring signals and threshold definitions are implemented. Production collector/exporter configuration, durable dashboards, log/trace retention, external notification routes and an alert-response rehearsal are environment/UAT acceptance work and cannot be truthfully certified from this repository.

### 11.51 SQL Server backup and isolated recovery drill

**Requirement:** R-53 / Phase 11 recovery hardening.

**Implementation:** `tools/Invoke-OpmsSqlServerRecoveryDrill.ps1` provides an executable full recovery rehearsal for the deployment database engine. The script accepts its connection string only through a named environment variable, never prints the credential, requires an explicit destructive-drill acknowledgement, and refuses every server except the local SQL Express aliases and every source database except `OPMS_Development` or `OPMS_IntegrationTests`. It performs a copy-only compressed backup with checksum, `RESTORE VERIFYONLY`, parses the backup file list, restores to a randomized isolated database/files, executes `DBCC CHECKDB`, evaluates configurable RPO/RTO thresholds, and cleans up only that exact isolated database unless retention was explicitly requested. A versioned JSON evidence record captures timing and outcomes under the ignored `artifacts/recovery-drills` directory.

The operational runbook is: stop or quiesce write-producing test activity; set `OPMS_RECOVERY_CONNECTION_STRING` in the invoking process from a local secret; ensure `sqlcmd` is installed and the SQL Server service account can write the selected backup directory; run `pwsh ./tools/Invoke-OpmsSqlServerRecoveryDrill.ps1 -ConfirmLocalDestructiveRecoveryDrill`; inspect the exit code and JSON evidence; investigate any backup, checksum, restore, integrity, cleanup, RPO or RTO failure before release. Production/UAT operators must substitute an infrastructure-approved encrypted/off-site backup schedule and isolated recovery environment—the repository script deliberately refuses remote or production databases.

**Files/classes:** `tools/Invoke-OpmsSqlServerRecoveryDrill.ps1`, `.github/workflows/quality.yml`, `.gitignore`, and `.env.example`.

**Migration:** None. The drill targets SQL Server backup semantics directly. Temporary SQLite development remains supported by application configuration, while moving to SQL Server requires configuration and existing EF migrations rather than domain, controller, service or UI changes.

**API:** None; recovery credentials and commands are intentionally excluded from HTTP surfaces.

**Tests:** `-ValidateOnly` emits the script's explicit server/database allowlist, safety gate and operation contract without reading credentials or mutating a database; CI executes this validation on every change. PowerShell parser validation and the non-mutating contract check pass locally. The live backup/restore branch was not executed because an approved reachable SQL Server test instance is not available in this run.

**Status:** R-53 advances from **NOT VERIFIABLE** to **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT**. The repository now contains a guarded and auditable recovery drill, but compliance requires retained evidence from successful scheduled backups, checksum verification, isolated restores, integrity checks and measured RPO/RTO in approved SQL Server infrastructure, plus a witnessed disaster-recovery/failover exercise.

### 11.52 Route-level loading and executable frontend performance budget

**Requirement:** Phase 11 performance hardening and R-54 automated quality enforcement.

**Implementation:** Authenticated feature modules are now loaded through React route-level `lazy` boundaries rather than being imported into the initial application graph. Authentication and access-denied paths remain immediately available, while each governed feature downloads only after the authorised route is selected. The common Suspense fallback is an announced status region. This reduces the production entry JavaScript from approximately **1,205 KiB** (**284 KiB gzip**) to approximately **289 KiB** (**79 KiB gzip**); the largest deferred feature chunk is the report/chart workspace at approximately **378 KiB** (**105 KiB gzip**).

`scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs` rejects a production build that collapses back to one JavaScript file or emits any JavaScript chunk above **450 KiB**. `npm run build:verify` combines the production build and budget, and CI now executes it as a required step. The budget is deliberately below the prior monolith while leaving controlled headroom for the largest charting route.

**Files/classes:** `ClientApp/src/App.tsx`, `ClientApp/scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs`, `ClientApp/package.json`, and `.github/workflows/quality.yml`.

**Migration:** None. Client performance behavior is independent of SQLite/SQL Server selection.

**API:** None.

**Tests:** TypeScript type-check and ESLint pass after converting every route component to a typed lazy import. The production build emits multiple route chunks, and `npm run check:bundle` verifies the **450 KiB** maximum against the actual build artifacts. The complete frontend and backend test suites remain required CI gates.

**Status:** Repository startup performance is materially hardened and regression-budgeted. Phase 11 performance remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending representative browser performance baselines, server/API load and saturation tests, slow-query/index analysis against native SQL Server, and bounded server pagination for remaining large collection endpoints.

### 11.53 Cookie-request integrity, API throttling and controlled seed secrets

**Requirement:** R-10 through R-13 and Phase 11 security hardening/testing.

**Implementation:** State-changing `/api` requests now require the `X-OPMS-Request: same-origin` browser header unless they use an explicit Bearer token. Combined with Secure production, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict authentication cookies and the existing allowlisted credentialed CORS policy, this blocks cross-site form/image mutation attempts while retaining non-browser API-client compatibility. The SPA sends the header on initial, upload, refresh and retried requests. Every controller endpoint also uses a fixed-window user/IP API policy in addition to the stricter authentication policy; rejected requests return 429 before controller work.

The security audit also removed the tracked `Credentials.md` plaintext credential dump and prevents it from being re-added. Controlled seeding no longer contains a password or administrator-email fallback. Administrator and optional demo-user passwords must be supplied through local/deployment secrets and meet the 12-character baseline. Demo identities require a separate `SeedData:DemoUsersEnabled` opt-in. Newly seeded identities must change their initial password, while an existing administrator or demo identity is left untouched—no password reset, profile rewrite, role rewrite or scope rewrite occurs on a later run. Seed creation failure is explicit rather than silently continuing with a partially governed identity.

**Files/classes:** `Infrastructure/Security/CsrfProtectionMiddleware.cs`, `Program.cs`, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/Seed/DbInitializer.cs`, `.env.example`, `.gitignore`, and `Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs`. The insecure tracked `Credentials.md` artifact is removed.

**Migration:** None. These controls are provider-neutral; deployment to SQL Server remains configuration-only for application code.

**API:** All unsafe `/api` methods enforce the same-origin header for cookie sessions; Bearer-token integrations remain supported. All mapped controllers inherit the `api` rate-limit policy, while authentication endpoints retain their stricter named policy.

**Tests:** Seven backend CSRF tests cover safe methods, missing-header rejection, accepted same-origin mutation, Bearer compatibility and non-API pass-through. Four controlled-secret tests reject missing/blank/short values and accept an explicit strong local secret. The cookie-session browser tests verify the header on login, refresh and retry while confirming that no Bearer token or token body is exposed. The complete backend suite passes **172/172** with one native SQL Server acceptance test explicitly skipped; the complete frontend suite passes **129/129** across **34** files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, production build and the 66-chunk bundle budget pass.

**Status:** R-10, R-11 and R-12 are **COMPLIANT** at repository level. R-13 advances to **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** because the current tree no longer contains the credential dump or seed defaults, but repository history and any environment that used the exposed values require owner-led credential rotation, optional history remediation, and production managed-secret injection evidence. External penetration testing and deployed proxy/TLS/cookie verification remain UAT acceptance work.

### 11.54 Bounded primary-register pagination

**Requirement:** R-45 / server-side pagination, filtering, sorting and search.

**Implementation:** OPMS targets, IPMS targets, OPMS submissions and IPMS submissions now expose additive `/api/v1/.../page` contracts. `PagedQueryRequest` validates page numbers, limits page size to 1-100, bounds search/sort input and accepts only ascending or descending order. Each controller applies the existing permission-derived record scope before search, `TotalCount`, sorting, `Skip` and `Take`; unauthorized records therefore cannot influence either results or metadata. Sort fields are explicit allowlists rather than client-provided expressions, and every sort ends with immutable `PublicId` as a deterministic tie-break. Response metadata includes page, page size, total count and total pages. Existing array contracts remain temporarily available for dashboard/detail compatibility.

**Files/classes:** `API/Requests/PagedQueryRequest.cs`, `API/Responses/PagedResponse.cs`, the four OPMS/IPMS target/submission controllers, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, and `ClientApp/src/types/index.ts`.

**Migration:** None. All queries use provider-neutral EF Core and the same tenant/security model for SQLite and SQL Server.

**Tests:** Six focused backend tests cover page bounds, metadata, stable server ordering, unknown-sort rejection, and scope-before-count behavior against relational SQLite. Frontend routing tests verify encoded bounded query parameters and versioned endpoints. The complete suites pass **184 backend tests** with one native SQL Server acceptance test skipped and **131 frontend tests** across 34 files; build, lint, type-check and bundle-budget gates pass.

Existing SPA register views and remaining compatibility/secondary endpoints that still load complete or fixed 200/500 collections must migrate before the rule is uniform, and representative SQL Server load/query-plan evidence remains outstanding.

### 11.55 General business-mutation idempotency

**Requirement:** R-46 / retry-safe POST mutations.

**Implementation:** Every authenticated non-authentication API `POST`, including JSON and multipart business mutations, now requires an `Idempotency-Key` containing 8-128 safe characters. The middleware runs after authentication, tenant resolution and authorization. It hashes the complete query/body stream, then atomically acquires a durable identity derived from municipality/system scope, user, method, normalized route and caller key. An identical completed request replays status, content type, location, ETag and body with `Idempotency-Replayed: true`; reuse with a changed body/query returns 409; an in-flight duplicate returns 409 plus `Retry-After`; and an exception after possible side effects is marked ambiguous and cannot be re-executed with the same key. Authentication endpoints are intentionally excluded because session-cookie responses must never be persisted or replayed.

`IdempotencyRequest` stores only request hashes—not request bodies—and retains replay bodies for a configurable 24-hour window. Records are tenant-filtered, FK-bound to user/municipality, concurrency protected, state constrained and uniquely indexed by a fixed SHA-256 identity hash, avoiding SQL Server composite-index width limits. A hosted cleanup worker removes expired records. The SPA generates one key per POST user action and reuses that exact key if cookie-session refresh causes an automatic retry. Swagger declares the required header on protected business POST operations.

**Files/classes:** `Domain/Entities/IdempotencyRequest.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/IdempotencyMiddleware.cs`, `Infrastructure/OpenApi/IdempotencyOperationFilter.cs`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `Program.cs`, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, and `appsettings.json`.

**Migration:** `20261002135324_V39IdempotentMutationRequests` creates the ledger with restricted user/municipality foreign keys, public-ID and fixed identity-hash uniqueness, expiry lookup, state check, response evidence and native SQL Server `rowversion`. SQLite uses the shared portable concurrency mechanism; deployment to SQL Server remains configuration plus migrations only.

**Tests:** Six focused relational/middleware tests prove single execution plus exact replay, payload and query conflict detection, missing/unsafe-key rejection, mandatory municipality resolution, municipality isolation, durable hashes, and fail-safe blocking after ambiguous exceptions. The browser session test proves one generated key survives a 401/refresh/retry sequence. Release build and the complete **184/184 backend** and **131/131 frontend** suites pass, with only the explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped. The complete idempotent SQL Server migration script generates at **376,498 bytes** and contains the ledger table, unique identity-hash index and generated `rowversion`.

**Status:** R-46 is **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Native SQL Server concurrent duplicate-request execution remains deployment acceptance evidence; no domain, controller, service or UI code changes are required when changing the configured database provider.

### 11.56 Municipality-configurable enterprise authentication

**Requirement:** R-09 / LOCAL, MICROSOFT_ENTRA_ID, ACTIVE_DIRECTORY and HYBRID authentication without a hard-coded provider.

**Implementation:** Each municipality can now persist one concurrency-governed `AuthenticationConfiguration` and `AuthenticationPolicy`. The mode controls whether local password sign-in is permitted and which deployment-registered enterprise provider may be challenged. Provider registrations are loaded from deployment configuration/secrets, require a unique safe code and callback path, accept only HTTPS authorities, and support Microsoft Entra ID or standards-based Active Directory federation through authorization-code OIDC with PKCE, issuer validation and short-lived correlation/nonce state. Client secrets are never stored in the application database or returned through an API. Switching between SQLite development and SQL Server deployment remains configuration-only; the domain, controller, service and UI code is shared.

External account linking is deliberately closed: an administrator must pre-provision a `UserAuthenticator` for an existing active user in the same municipality. A validated callback first resolves an existing fixed provider/issuer/subject digest; an unbound pre-provision may bind exactly once only when its expected email matches the validated claim. Email alone never creates a user or links an arbitrary account. Unique hashes reject duplicate external identities, disabled and cross-municipality accounts are denied, and authentication changes use `RowVersion`. `AuthenticationEvent` is tenant-filtered and append-only; configuration, provisioning and status changes also append the normal audit trail with the governance reason. External success issues the same hashed-refresh, database-governed internal session used by local authentication.

The public login screen discovers only the municipality display name, whether local sign-in is enabled, and non-secret provider code/name/kind. It redirects to a fixed provider challenge and safely resumes the cookie-only session after the fixed local callback. The lazy-loaded Authentication Governance page selects the mode/provider, preserves policy values, pre-provisions users, enables/disables authenticators and displays recent authentication events. Dynamic permissions `AUTHENTICATION.READ`, `AUTHENTICATION.CONFIGURE`, `AUTHENTICATION.LINK_IDENTITIES` and `AUTHENTICATION.VIEW_EVENTS`, plus a permission-protected navigation item, keep UI and direct API enforcement aligned.

**Files/classes:** `Domain/Entities/EnterpriseAuthenticationEntities.cs`, `Infrastructure/Auth/EnterpriseAuthenticationOptions.cs`, `Infrastructure/Auth/EnterpriseAuthenticationService.cs`, `API/Controllers/EnterpriseAuthController.cs`, `API/Controllers/AuthenticationAdministrationController.cs`, `API/Controllers/AuthController.cs`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/Seed/SecurityRegistrySeeder.cs`, `Program.cs`, `ClientApp/src/components/auth/Login.tsx`, `ClientApp/src/components/security/AuthenticationAdministration.tsx`, `ClientApp/src/context/AppContext.tsx`, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, and `appsettings.json`.

**Migration:** `20261002141303_V39EnterpriseAuthentication` creates configurations, policies, authenticators and append-only events with restricted tenant/user foreign keys, unique municipality configuration/policy, fixed external-identity hash uniqueness, effective-date and policy-bound checks, and native SQL Server `rowversion`. Relational SQLite tests exercise the same EF model and constraints through schema creation. The historical migration chain remains SQL Server-authored and is not claimed to be portable as a from-zero SQLite migration; SQL Server deployment uses the committed migrations with no application-code changes.

**Tests:** Four focused tests reject unsafe provider registrations, prove single pre-provisioned binding, prove that unknown email claims cannot auto-create/link accounts, verify SQLite external-identity uniqueness, and enforce append-only authentication events. The full backend suite passes **188/188**, with the environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped; the frontend suite passes **132/132** across 34 files. TypeScript type-check, ESLint, focused login UI tests, production frontend build, the 66-chunk bundle budget, backend build, model/snapshot consistency, and generation/inspection of the **388,378-byte** idempotent SQL Server script pass.

**Status:** R-09 is **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Production acceptance still requires approved provider registrations/secrets, live Entra ID and AD-federation redirect/claim tests, conditional-access/MFA behavior, provider outage/fallback behavior, logout/revocation testing, and security-owner sign-off. Section 11.58 closes the remaining repository-level dynamic authentication-policy gap.

### 11.57 Immutable contextual audit trails

**Requirement:** R-38 / immutable, queryable audit evidence with actor, tenant, reason, request and session context.

**Implementation:** `AuditTrail` was already protected by the central tenant write guard, tenant query filter, restricted municipality/user foreign keys and application-level append-only enforcement. The central workflow governance writer now also captures the resolved municipality, validated correlation identifier, governed JWT session identifier, user agent, IP address and optional governance reason alongside the existing public ID, entity/action, old/new JSON snapshots, actor and timestamp. This context is server-derived from the current request rather than accepted from a browser payload. Existing module-specific writers that supply a reason remain compatible.

The audit query now has a versioned `/api/v1/audit/trails` route and requires `Audit.Trails.View` at the controller, closing the previous direct-API bypass where the UI checked permission but the endpoint required only authentication. Responses expose the complete contextual evidence without changing stored values. The legacy read route remains temporarily available under the same permission for compatibility. Audit records remain non-updatable and non-deletable through `ApplicationDbContext`.

**Files/classes:** `Domain/Entities/WorkflowGovernanceEntities.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/WorkflowGovernanceService.cs`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `API/Controllers/AuditController.cs`, `API/Controllers/PerformanceApiSupport.cs`, `API/Responses/AuthResponses.cs`, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, and `ClientApp/src/types/index.ts`.

**Migration:** `20261002142601_V39AuditContextEvidence` adds the bounded session identifier and applies bounded context columns. Before narrowing any legacy column, SQL Server executes an explicit preflight and throws if an existing value exceeds the new bound; it never silently truncates audit evidence. The generated idempotent SQL Server script contains the preflight and session column. SQLite relational tests continue to exercise the shared model and append-only behavior through schema creation; deployment to SQL Server requires only configuration and the committed migrations.

**Tests:** A focused service test proves tenant, correlation, session, user-agent, IP, reason and before/after capture. A controller metadata test proves the dynamic permission and versioned route, and the frontend route test proves the SPA uses that route. Existing tenant tests prove stamping/filtering and reject mutation of a persisted trail. The complete suites pass **190/190 backend** and **133/133 frontend** tests, with only the explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped. Backend/frontend builds, TypeScript, ESLint, bundle budget, model/snapshot consistency and the inspected **392,641-byte** SQL Server script pass.

**Status:** R-38 is **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Deployed acceptance must still verify privileged audit-role assignment, retention/export procedures, database-account least privilege, log/SIEM forwarding and evidentiary retention policy.

### 11.58 Dynamic municipality authentication-policy enforcement

**Requirement:** R-10 through R-12 / municipality-governed password, MFA, lockout and session controls that take effect without recompilation.

**Implementation:** The stored, concurrency-governed `AuthenticationPolicy` is now resolved for the authenticated user's municipality on every relevant operation. Local Identity password creation, reset and change use the municipality minimum length in addition to the global complexity and compromised-password validators. Failed local sign-in uses the configured attempt threshold and lockout duration; the global Identity threshold is retained only as a safe ceiling above the permitted municipality range. New tenant users inherit the configured first-login password-change requirement without rewriting existing administrator-managed accounts.

JWT/session issuance and validation apply the current municipality idle timeout, absolute lifetime and maximum concurrent-session count. A stricter policy therefore affects effective session validation without recompilation. Each refresh-token family records a bounded authentication method, exposes it in account session administration and preserves it through rotation. Local TOTP enrollment is required for all or only privileged local users according to policy; an externally authenticated Entra ID or AD-federated session is not incorrectly forced into the local TOTP flow. Access tokens retain the normalized authentication-method claim. Existing refresh sessions are conservatively backfilled as `LOCAL` during upgrade.

The Authentication Governance UI now edits every bounded policy value rather than silently preserving hidden defaults: minimum password length, failed-attempt threshold, lockout duration, privileged/all-local MFA, first-login password change, idle timeout, absolute timeout and concurrent-session maximum. RowVersion and governance-reason handling remain on the existing administration contract. The account settings session list identifies the authentication method used for each active session.

**Files/classes:** `Infrastructure/Auth/AuthenticationPolicyResolver.cs`, `Infrastructure/Auth/JwtService.cs`, `API/Controllers/AuthController.cs`, `API/Controllers/EnterpriseAuthController.cs`, `Domain/Entities/RefreshToken.cs`, `Infrastructure/Persistence/ApplicationDbContext.cs`, `Program.cs`, `ClientApp/src/components/security/AuthenticationAdministration.tsx`, `ClientApp/src/components/settings/Settings.tsx`, and `ClientApp/src/types/index.ts`.

**Migration:** `20261002143341_V39DynamicAuthenticationPolicy` adds the bounded non-null `RefreshTokens.AuthenticationMethod` column and backfills existing families as `LOCAL`. The generated idempotent SQL Server script uses `nvarchar(40) NOT NULL DEFAULT N'LOCAL'`. SQLite relational tests use the identical EF model through schema creation; SQL Server deployment remains configuration plus committed migrations only, with no domain, controller, service or UI fork.

**Tests:** Resolver tests prove current tenant-policy selection and safe configuration fallback. Password validation tests prove municipality length enforcement. Policy tests prove that local MFA applies to all/privileged users as configured while enterprise sessions are exempt from local enrollment. JWT/session tests prove dynamic absolute lifetime, one-session enforcement, provider-method persistence and claims. The frontend administration test proves that stored values load and changed values are submitted, while account-session tests exercise the authentication-method response. The full suites pass **194/194 backend** and **134/134 frontend** tests across **35** frontend files, with only the explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped. Release build, TypeScript type-check, ESLint, production frontend build, 66-chunk bundle budget, model/snapshot consistency and inspection of the **393,234-byte** idempotent SQL Server script pass.

**Status:** R-10, R-11 and R-12, including the broader municipality authentication-policy row, are **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Native SQL Server execution, real-provider federation/conditional-access tests, threshold tuning from production telemetry, browser penetration testing and operational support procedures remain deployment/UAT acceptance evidence.

### 11.59 Standard RFC 7807 API errors and validation

**Requirement:** R-44 and the API contract baseline / consistent 400, 401, 403, 404, 409, 413, 415, 422, 429 and 500 behavior.

**Implementation:** Successful API calls retain the established typed `ApiResponse<T>` contract. A global always-run MVC result filter now converts every failed legacy `ApiResponse<T>` returned by any controller into RFC 7807 `ProblemDetails` without requiring hundreds of error paths to be rewritten independently. Automatic `[ApiController]` binding, non-nullability and DataAnnotations failures use field-keyed `ValidationProblemDetails`. Empty framework authentication, authorization, routing, media-type, payload-size and rate-limit responses are filled by the centralized status-code pipeline. CSRF, tenant resolution, password-change, MFA-enrollment and idempotency middleware use the same writer.

Every problem carries status, title, request instance, a stable machine code and the validated request correlation ID. Required mappings include `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, `ACCESS_DENIED`, `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, `STATE_CONFLICT`, `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, `UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`, `BUSINESS_RULE_VIOLATION`, `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and `INTERNAL_ERROR`; controlled middleware may supply a more specific code without central customization overwriting it. The exception handler maps explicit authorization, concurrency and application-validation exceptions, while unexpected exceptions—including framework `ArgumentException` failures—return a sanitized 500 and never expose infrastructure detail. The exception/status pipeline now wraps all downstream security, telemetry, CSRF, authentication, tenant, authorization and controller processing.

Swagger adds `application/problem+json` documentation for the standard failure statuses on every operation. The SPA transport already supported ProblemDetails and now also preserves stable code and body/header correlation evidence while flattening field errors into the existing UI error list. Existing screens therefore remain compatible as the server failure shape becomes uniform.

**Files/classes:** `Infrastructure/Security/ApiProblemDetails.cs`, `Infrastructure/OpenApi/ProblemDetailsOperationFilter.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/CorrelationIdMiddleware.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/CsrfProtectionMiddleware.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/TenantContext.cs`, `Infrastructure/Security/IdempotencyMiddleware.cs`, `Infrastructure/Auth/MfaRequirementPolicy.cs`, `Infrastructure/Auth/PasswordChangePolicy.cs`, `API/Responses/AuthResponses.cs`, `Program.cs`, `ClientApp/src/api/api.ts`, and `ClientApp/src/types/index.ts`.

**Migration:** None. The contract is provider-neutral and requires no database, domain, controller, service or UI variation between SQLite development and SQL Server deployment.

**Tests:** Filter tests prove legacy failure conversion and success-response preservation. Model-state tests prove field-keyed validation. Status-code theories cover all required mappings. Middleware tests prove RFC 7807 content, specific-code preservation and correlation evidence. Exception tests prove unexpected argument/configuration detail is sanitized as a 500. The client test proves detail, code, flattened validation messages and correlation evidence survive transport normalization. A disposable SQLite-hosted HTTP smoke run exercised the real routing/authentication/filter/formatter stack and returned: 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with `Email`/`Password` field errors, 400 `CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`, and 401 `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, all as `application/problem+json` with the caller's correlation ID. Disposable processes and database files were removed afterward. The full suites pass **209/209 backend** and **135/135 frontend** tests, with only the explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server acceptance test skipped. Release build, TypeScript, ESLint, production build, 66-chunk bundle budget and model/snapshot consistency pass.

**Status:** R-44 is **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Contract consumers should use the stable problem code rather than matching human-readable detail. Deployed gateway/proxy behavior and external integration conformance remain UAT acceptance evidence.

### 11.60 Server-paged primary SPA registers

In server mode it renders the supplied page without slicing it again, displays the authoritative total count, delegates page selection, exposes only columns with an allow-listed API sort key, and delegates search text. OPMS/IPMS target and submission register pages now call their versioned `/page` APIs with a fixed page size of 25, debounce server search, reset to page one on search/sort changes, retain stable current-page mutations, and show server totals rather than the number of rows currently held in browser memory. The server continues to apply dynamic authorization scope before search, count, ordering and paging.

The existing OPMS advanced filter panel remains a transparent current-page refinement and reports “shown of total” when active; it is not represented as a server-wide filter. Submission creation still uses its compatibility target selector. Those selectors and secondary/fixed-limit collections are intentionally retained in the R-45 gap rather than overstating uniform compliance.

Backend behavior is unchanged from the previously verified **209/209 tests**, with only the explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server acceptance test skipped.

### 11.65 Dynamic authorization cutover, canonical submission base state, and local SQLite profile

**Requirement:** R-08, R-24, R-27 and the XAF-style dynamic-security/local-database implementation prompts.

**Implementation:** The SPA no longer owns a route-to-permission matrix or a role-name dashboard matrix. Route access and dashboard work areas now derive from the server-filtered navigation tree; the unused hard-coded menu builder was removed. The registry includes the self-service Settings route, and operation/member controls continue to use stable effective permission codes. Employee email now provides a second end-to-end member-security implementation: API list projections redact it without `EMPLOYEE.EmailAddress.READ`, mutations reject attempted changes without `EMPLOYEE.EmailAddress.UPDATE`, and the administration form hides or locks the control from the same permissions. Submission member enforcement remains in the OPMS/IPMS controllers.

OPMS and IPMS submissions now carry a database-constrained base state with only `IN_PROGRESS` and `SUBMITTED`. New and returned submissions use `IN_PROGRESS`; submit advances the base state; later configured stages retain `SUBMITTED`. The API and SPA expose the base state independently from the configured stage/status label. Migration `20261002204550_V39CanonicalSubmissionBaseState` backfills legacy draft/rejected values to `IN_PROGRESS` and all post-submission stages to `SUBMITTED`. The fixed transition validator was removed: no effective tenant/year/type workflow now returns a conflict and creates no workflow instance or action.

Development configuration selects `Sqlite` at `App_Data/opms-development.db` with development-only schema creation, while default/production configuration remains SQL Server. The data directory is retained without tracking database files. Dynamic-security integration tests now use relational SQLite, exercising real foreign keys rather than EF InMemory behavior.

The TID workspace now consumes the bounded page endpoint, uses authoritative totals and has a corrected component test contract. The existing governed HR routes continue to resolve to tenant employee/organization administration rather than prototype screens.

**Tests:** Relational SQLite security coverage passes default deny, multi-role explicit-DENY precedence, restriction-only overrides, record/assignment scope, navigation filtering/registry integrity, role-assignment hierarchy, employee member redaction/direct-update denial, and missing-workflow fail-closed behavior. Canonical base-state regression theories cover current and legacy values. TID controller coverage proves bounded metadata/current-version projection and unknown-sort rejection; route and component tests prove exact page transport, authoritative totals and next-page navigation. The complete backend suite passes **228/228** with one environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped; the frontend suite passes **131/131 across 37 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, production build, the 66-chunk bundle budget and `git diff --check` pass.

**Status:** The repository-side dynamic navigation, CRUD/action evaluation, scoped authorization, submission/employee member enforcement, configurable workflow fail-closed behavior and temporary SQLite development profile are implemented. R-08 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** because member catalog/enforcement still needs expansion when additional sensitive fields are introduced and deployment-level HTTP penetration/privilege-escalation acceptance remains external evidence. R-24 and R-27 are repository-complete. R-45 remains partial only for compatibility/detail selectors and native SQL Server performance acceptance; the earlier TID fixed-limit gap is closed.

### 11.66 Central performance-consolidation compatibility engine

**Requirement:** V3.9 sections 5.6, 11.7-11.10 and 15.1; final assessment area 9.

**Implementation:** `PerformanceConsolidationEngine` is the single server-side authority for determining whether source-period values may safely produce a Mid-Term or Annual suggestion. It implements the nine specified calculation types. `SUM` and `AVERAGE` perform canonical numeric aggregation; `LATEST_VALUE` selects the latest canonical source; `CUMULATIVE` and `REVERSE_CUMULATIVE` default to the latest valid cumulative position. `NON_CUMULATIVE`, `REVERSE_NON_CUMULATIVE`, `ZERO_BASED` and `MANUAL` fail closed unless an explicit municipality rule selects a safe primitive operation. An explicit configured rule may also override a cumulative default without recompilation. `PerformanceTargetDerivationService` applies the V3.9 Mid-Term precedence rule: a valid approved source Mid-Term target wins without derivation metadata; when it is absent, Q1 and Q2 flow through the central engine and return the canonical target, `IsSystemDerivedTarget`, source-period list, effective derivation rule and immutable original system-suggested value needed by persistence.

The engine accepts an ordered required-period set and only consumes submitted, effective source values. Missing values default to blocking and are never silently converted to zero; explicit block, manual, ignore and treat-as-zero policies are represented separately. Source and destination units must match until an explicit conversion rule exists. Every value and generated result is passed through the canonical performance-unit engine. Mathematical aggregation rejects dates, ratios, qualitative and binary values, while latest-value selection can safely preserve their canonical representation. Every refusal returns a stable code and user-facing explanation suitable for the frontend rather than requiring duplicated React calculation logic.

Persistence now follows the V3.9 ownership model. Nine stable calculation-type masters are seeded and referenced by OPMS/IPMS KPIs. A tenant-filtered municipality policy stores an optional safe primitive override, explicit missing-value policy, derived-target override permission, annual-target derivation flag and provider-portable `RowVersion`. Normalized period targets store `IsSystemDerivedTarget`, source periods, the effective calculation type and the original system-suggested target. OPMS/IPMS submissions store `SystemSuggestedActualPerformance` separately from final `ActualPerformance`, the edited flag, calculation type, generation timestamp and editor/time/reason metadata. `PerformanceSuggestionEvent` is a tenant-owned append-only ledger for generated, accepted and edited values with source periods, actor and correlation evidence. Database checks require exactly one OPMS/IPMS submission per event and complete edit metadata whenever the edited flag is set.

Versioned routes under `/api/v1/performance-consolidation` expose the active calculation-type catalogue and the current tenant's complete policy projection. Policy creation/update requires the dynamic `OPMS_KPI.CONFIGURE_CONSOLIDATION` action, a municipality context and an explicit governance reason. The server permits only `SUM`, `AVERAGE` or `LATEST_VALUE` as configured primitive overrides, validates the missing-value enum, protects updates with `RowVersion`, returns conflicts for stale or duplicate writes and writes contextual create/edit audit evidence. Read routes independently require `OPMS_KPI.READ`; hiding a future administration view therefore cannot bypass direct API authorization.

The OPMS and IPMS submission routes now complete the runtime path. Mid-Term generation resolves exactly Q1 and Q2; Annual generation resolves exactly Q1-Q4. Only canonical `SUBMITTED`, timestamped, active source submissions participate. Missing or duplicate period configuration, missing source values, incompatible units, invalid destination targets, withdrawn/non-editable submissions and unsafe calculation rules fail closed with stable explanations. Generation is idempotent: the first suggestion is retained, concurrent generation is protected by submission `RowVersion`, and later attempts do not create duplicate ledger entries. Accepting the suggestion or saving an authorised deviation recalculates canonical actual, variance, achievement percentage and target-achieved state from the governed destination period target. A deviation requires a reason and never overwrites the original suggestion.

Versioned OPMS/IPMS endpoints generate suggestions, save final consolidated actuals and return ordered immutable history. Entity UPDATE/READ and `ActualPerformance` member permissions are independently checked against the submission scope, so a hidden control cannot authorize a direct request. Suggestion fields are redacted with the protected actual member. The submission workspace displays the original suggestion, final actual, generated/edited metadata, edit reason and event history for Mid-Term and Annual periods; authorised owners can generate, accept or edit, while client controls enforce the reason rule and the API remains authoritative.

**Tests:** Thirty-one engine/derivation tests cover the defined behavior of all nine types, configured municipality rules, default missing-value protection, explicit ignore/zero handling, submitted/effective eligibility, incompatible units, invalid sources, unsafe nonnumeric aggregation, canonical nonnumeric latest values, destination-unit result validation, duplicate-period rejection, explicit-target precedence, complete derived-target metadata and invalid-explicit-target refusal. Three relational persistence tests prove all nine seeded codes, tenant-policy isolation and uniqueness, portable RowVersion concurrency, separate suggestion/final fields and append-only history. Six policy-controller tests prove authorized create/update, RowVersion transport, contextual audit, the complete policy projection, rejection of unsafe overrides and direct-call denial without the dynamic permission. Four relational runtime tests prove Mid-Term sum, Annual average, exact source-period ordering, missing-quarter refusal without mutation, one-time generation, acceptance/edit history, preservation of the original suggestion, metric recalculation and direct API denial when member permission is absent. The complete backend suite passes **281/281** with one explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server acceptance test skipped. The frontend suite passes **140/140 across 42 files**; TypeScript and ESLint pass. The Release host build passes with zero warnings and zero errors. SQL Server model/snapshot consistency passes, and the **417,488-byte** idempotent migration script contains the new tables, fields, checks, indexes, seed data and restricted foreign keys.
