### 11.67 Configurable Internal Audit assessment models

**Requirement:** V3.9 sections 4.10A, 17.3 and 18.1, including the acceptance scenarios in the Internal Audit assessment-model matrix; final assessment areas 13 and 15.

**Implementation:** `InternalAuditAssessmentConfiguration` is an effective-dated, tenant-owned, municipality-financial-year-specific version record. Administrators select either Detailed IA Assessment or Satisfactory / Not Satisfactory through Workflow Governance. Replacing a selection requires the current RowVersion, closes the predecessor, appends a successor and writes contextual audit evidence; the database enforces one current selection and unique version numbers. Existing submissions resolve the applicable model through their pinned workflow definition and financial year, so a later selection never rewrites an earlier assessment.

`InternalAuditAssessment` is an append-only assessment/reassessment ledger linked to the exact configuration and workflow instance. A reassessment must reference the latest prior assessment, while a filtered unique successor constraint prevents competing branches. The simplified model accepts exactly IA Assessment Status and required IA Detailed Observation. `NOT_SATISFACTORY` requires a future response due date and atomically creates a scoped `PerformanceRfi`, Internal Audit notification/outbox evidence and workflow actions. A later `SATISFACTORY` assessment appends a new version without changing the original adverse assessment or RFI. The Detailed model accepts IA Achievement / IA Not Achieved plus optional comments, findings, recommendation and score. An adverse detailed outcome follows the same mandatory IA-RFI rule. When the configured Audit workflow stage requires a rating, the supplied score is mandatory, must exist in its rating scheme and also appends the normal stage-rating ledger entry.

Versioned `/api/v1/internal-audit` routes expose model history, resolved submission model/history and assessment creation. Configuration uses the dynamic `WORKFLOW.CONFIGURE` policy. Assessment mutation independently checks the OPMS/IPMS `INTERNAL_AUDIT` action through the central scope engine; history reads require the scoped submission READ permission, so hiding an auditor control cannot authorize a direct request. Tenant query filters and write guards cover both new tables. Configuration deletion and assessment mutation/deletion are rejected by DbContext governance.

The Workflow Governance SPA has an Internal Audit tab for tenant/year model selection and complete version history. The auditor queue adds the model-specific form to the selected submission. The simplified form renders only the two specified assessment fields; an adverse choice conditionally requires the IA RFI Due Date. The Detailed form exposes its permitted narratives and score. Both show ordered immutable assessment/RFI history, actor/time evidence and reassessment behavior.

**Migration and tests:** SQL Server migration `20261003145227_V39InternalAuditAssessmentModels` adds both tenant-owned tables, restricted foreign keys, native RowVersion for configuration, date checks, public/business-key indexes, one-current-selection uniqueness and reassessment-chain uniqueness. Six focused relational/controller tests prove model versioning, audit and RowVersion transport, exact simplified-field validation, mandatory adverse due date, atomic RFI creation, configured rating-scheme enforcement, append-only reassessment, direct API denial and immutable history. Eight focused frontend tests across the assessment, queue and governance surfaces pass. The complete backend suite passes **287/287** with one explicitly environment-gated native SQL Server acceptance test skipped; the complete frontend suite passes **142/142 across 43 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, production build, SQL Server model/snapshot consistency and `git diff --check` pass.

**Status:** Both required Internal Audit assessment models, tenant/year selection, model-specific validation, RFI/workflow/rating integration, append-only history and production SPA capture are **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Official municipality report formats remain assessed separately under area 20, and native SQL Server execution remains area 31.

### 11.68 Official municipality report generation and immutable version history

The former transient-only performance export path is now complemented by a governed official-report subsystem aligned to sections 4.14, 32.13 and 36 of the authoritative specification:

- `OfficialReportTemplate` preserves municipality-owned, optionally financial-year-specific approved template families with immutable version lineage, effective dates, approval reference, reason, output format, heading tokens, configured columns and `RowVersion` concurrency. A successor closes the prior current version without deleting it.
- `OfficialReportGeneration` is append-only and pins Municipality, Municipality Financial Year, reporting period, template/version, OPMS/IPMS framework, authorization scope, filters, generator/timestamp, row count, SHA-256 output hash and deterministic data-version reference. Regeneration appends within the same family; prior files remain addressable.
- One canonical authorized dataset feeds CSV, XLSX, DOCX and paginated PDF renderers. Spreadsheet formula prefixes are neutralized, Office outputs use Open XML packages, PDF retains every row across pages, and deterministic snapshot/content hashes distinguish data state from download time.
- Generated output uses the private `IEvidenceBlobStorage` abstraction and tenant-owned `EvidenceBlob` metadata marked system-generated. Every download re-evaluates dynamic read permission, proves the stored scope is not broader than the caller's current scope and verifies SHA-256 integrity before returning content. Generation, download and integrity failures are audit logged.
- `/api/v1/reports/official/templates`, `/generations` and `/generations/{PublicId}/content` independently enforce dynamic `READ`, `GENERATE` and new `CONFIGURE` permissions. Template versioning and generation retain tenant/FY/period FK integrity and fail closed when context or permission is absent.
- The Performance Reports SPA provides approved-template selection, FY/period generation, immutable history, secured download, regeneration, snapshot references and permission-controlled template administration. The existing ad-hoc CSV remains transient and is not represented as official.
- Migration `20261003152131_V39OfficialReportGeneration` contains only the template/generation tables, filtered current-version and lineage uniqueness, restricted FKs, checks and SQL Server `rowversion`. The EF snapshot matches and an idempotent SQL Server increment script generates successfully.
- `OfficialReportGenerationTests` exercise all four deterministic formats, formula neutralization, PDF multi-page completeness, invalid-layout rejection, relational tenant filtering, append-only protection and cross-tenant write denial. The report workspace test covers the official history surface.

**Status:** official OPMS/IPMS performance outputs and immutable version history are **COMPLIANT at repository level**. ### 11.69 Configurable reminder, escalation, working-calendar and SMS policies

The remaining repository-controlled notification-policy gap is closed:

- `NotificationConfiguration` and immutable child schedule rules provide municipality/FY-owned version families with Draft → Active → Superseded lifecycle, effective dates, municipality-default → workflow-stage-default → reporting-period-override inheritance, OPMS/IPMS scope, reporting-window or RFI deadlines, mandatory/optional classification, channel selection, controlled templates, audit actors and SQL Server `rowversion` concurrency. A filtered unique index permits at most one active policy for an exact applicability key.
- Working-day offsets are configuration, not hard-coded behavior. Negative offsets create reminders, zero represents due day and positive offsets create overdue escalations. `WorkingCalendarHoliday` combines municipality-configured South African public holidays with automatic weekend exclusion. Copy-to-financial-year creates a Draft successor, maps equivalent reporting periods and recalculates effective dates before activation.
- `NotificationScheduleWorker` performs multi-tenant downtime catch-up. Materialized `ScheduledNotification` records use a unique deterministic logical key. Changes to an effective policy/template/deadline supersede stale unsent materializations; already queued/sent evidence is preserved. Pausing delivery leaves workflow deadlines untouched, and resuming catches up due work.
- Recipients resolve dynamically from primary KPI assignees, effective role assignments or explicit governed users. Reporting-period and workflow-stage inheritance is evaluated centrally. Manual processing from the administration API is restricted to the selected municipality while the hosted worker processes every tenant independently.
- The outbox supports policy-pinned `IN_APP`, `EMAIL` and `SMS` channels. `HttpSmsNotificationSender` is provider-neutral and uses the same idempotency and receipt ledger as email. Missing addresses are terminal `NotDeliverable` outcomes and do not retry forever; an in-app delivery in the same event remains successful. Transient provider failures retain bounded retry/backoff behavior.
- End-user optional email/SMS/digest preferences are now persisted through authenticated APIs instead of component-local placeholder state. Optional channel delivery records a terminal `Suppressed` receipt when disabled. Mandatory workflow, deadline, RFI and escalation policies explicitly bypass preferences and always retain in-app evidence.
- Workflow Governance now includes policy drafting, activation, inherited scope, working-day offsets, role/user/assignee recipients, channel selection, controlled placeholders, preview, provider-pipeline test, pause/resume, prior-FY copy, due-run and working-calendar administration. All management routes require the dynamic `WORKFLOW.CONFIGURE` permission, so hiding the UI never authorizes a direct request.
- Migrations `20261003160701_V39NotificationPoliciesAndScheduling` and `20261003161402_V39NotificationPreferences` create only the policy/rule/calendar/materialization/preference structures with restricted foreign keys, uniqueness, checks, filtered active-policy enforcement and native SQL Server `rowversion`. The application model remains provider-neutral for SQLite development and SQL Server deployment.

Focused relational and worker tests prove weekend/holiday calculations, controlled placeholders, mandatory in-app inclusion, downtime catch-up, logical idempotency, pause/resume behavior, period-override precedence, dynamic endpoint protection, SMS readiness, missing-address terminal behavior and optional-preference suppression versus mandatory bypass. Production UI tests prove policy/calendar administration and persisted self-service preferences. The complete suites pass **304 backend tests** with one environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped and **144/144 frontend tests across 43 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, frontend/Release builds, the 66-chunk bundle budget, model/snapshot consistency, the inspected **451,703-byte** idempotent SQL Server script and `git diff --check` pass.

### 11.70 Governed legacy-target reconciliation console

The controlled target-normalization API is now exposed as an administrator-operable production workflow rather than a backend-only capability:

- Only `Ready` records can be selected. Blocked records retain the server's specific governance explanation, including ambiguous legacy revised values that require a reasoned governed revision rather than an unsafe automatic overwrite. Already-normalized records are visible but immutable through the cutover action.
- Execution requires `OPMS_KPI.NORMALIZE_LEGACY`, at least one explicit target public ID and a governance reason. The existing API revalidates all selected records inside the transaction, adds only missing `PerformancePeriodTarget` rows, attributes them to the authenticated operator and writes audit evidence. Re-running the same selection is idempotent.
- Paging is bounded to 50 records, register selection is explicit, and changing between OPMS and IPMS clears stale selection. The UI never offers an unbounded or silent “migrate everything” operation.
- Component tests prove readiness rendering, blocked-selection enforcement, exact reason/identifier transport, success evidence and OPMS/IPMS register switching. The Workflow Governance integration test proves the production route opens the cutover console.

### 11.71 Canonical ActualPerformance contract and lossless legacy retirement

The remaining repository-side competing-actual representation is retired without discarding historic data:

Server-derived variance, achievement and target-achieved results remain response evidence, not client-owned inputs.
- Submission controllers pass only `ActualPerformance` to the central unit engine, persist its canonical value and derive metrics from the matching normalized period target. Member security continues to protect the canonical value and variance narrative; unauthorized responses redact `ActualPerformance`, expenditure, derived metrics and suggestion evidence together.
- Production submission forms and the shared workspace capture `ActualPerformance` as text so numeric, percentage, date, time, binary, ratio and qualitative units can all use the same canonical contract. The former numeric-only actual, client due-date, client variance and separate performance-description inputs are no longer presented as authoritative capture fields.
- `V39CanonicalActualPerformanceCutover` creates `LegacySubmissionValueArchives` before removing any column. It archives each OPMS/IPMS legacy value and narrative with submission linkage, municipality where recoverable, canonical value, UTC timestamp and reason; backfills missing canonical numeric values; then drops the six retired columns. The downgrade recreates and restores all archived legacy fields before removing the archive table.
- Archive rows are tenant-filtered, relationally constrained, unique per submission and append-only at the persistence boundary. Nullable municipality ownership is intentionally retained only for unreconciled historic orphan rows so the migration never discards them; all governed runtime writes still require tenant ownership.
- Contract/model tests prove all four public request/response types and both submission entities lack the competing fields, and relational tests prove archive linkage plus append-only enforcement. The generated idempotent SQL orders archive inserts and canonical backfill before `DROP COLUMN`.

### 11.79 Tenant-scoped dynamic user administration and protected contact members

The legacy user-administration boundary now participates in the database-driven security model instead of treating one broad UI permission as authority for every operation:

### 11.81 Period-based KPI ordering and governed resequencing

The V3.9 SDBIP sequencing rule is now persisted and enforced rather than inferred from indicator text:

- OPMS and IPMS KPIs carry independent positive `OriginalOrderNumber` and `RevisedOrderNumber` values. New records default revised order to original order, while migration `20261004031802_V39PeriodBasedKpiOrdering` deterministically backfills both values per municipality using creation time and stable public ID before adding constraints and tenant/order indexes.
- Effective-order register queries require an explicit reporting-period type. Q1, Q2 and Mid-Term sort by original order; Q3, Q4 and Annual sort by revised order. Duplicate business order values are permitted and use `PublicId` as the deterministic tie-breaker.
- Performance CSV export rows use the same period-effective rule rather than indicator-number sorting. The rule applies independently of the generic `IsRevised` flag, so an otherwise unchanged KPI can move in the approved revised SDBIP sequence.
- OPMS/IPMS ordering changes use dedicated permission- and scope-protected endpoints with mandatory reason, approval reference, effective date and RowVersion. Each changed order field appends an immutable tenant-owned `KpiFieldRevision`; stale writes fail with conflict and withdrawn KPIs remain immutable.
- The KPI detail UI explains the two period regimes, exposes both values only as editable controls to users with the relevant dynamic UPDATE capability, and displays immutable revision history to authorized readers.
- Six relational tests prove the original/revised switch in reports and paged registers, mandatory period context, OPMS/IPMS field-specific append-only history, stale RowVersion rejection, cross-tenant not-found behavior, positive ordering constraints and unambiguous revision ownership. A component test proves the governed revision payload and history experience.

The complete suites pass **326 backend tests**, with one environment-gated SQL Server test skipped, and **157/157 frontend tests across 49 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, the Release/backend and production/frontend builds, model consistency and idempotent SQL generation pass. Area 10 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** only because equivalent field-specific governed revision ledgers are not yet complete for every independently revisable KPI metadata field; the period-based ordering requirement itself is repository-complete.

### 11.82 Independent KPI-definition and period-value revisions

The remaining repository-level V3.9 revision gap is closed without reusing the ambiguous generic `IsRevised` switch:

- OPMS and IPMS KPIs persist independent flags and revised values for KPI number, target name and KPI wording while retaining each original. Canonical period rows independently persist revised target/unit and revised applicable budget state.
- A central resolver applies originals to Q1, Q2 and Mid-Term and only independently flagged values to Q3, Q4 and Annual. Registers, selectors, submission pages, workflow queues, consolidation calculations, transient exports and immutable official report generation use the same period-aware rule.
- General KPI edits cannot overwrite original KPI number/name/wording or canonical period values. Dedicated OPMS/IPMS revision endpoints require the dynamic `*.REVISE` action, record external approval metadata, apply tenant/record scope, reject meaningless revised-equals-original values, protect writes with RowVersion and append immutable field history. Ordering and definition histories are returned separately.
- Q1/Q2/Mid-Term target/unit/budget revision flags are rejected server-side. Late-period target and budget flags remain independent, original values are never overwritten, and removing a flag returns the effective value to its original through another auditable revision event.
- The KPI detail UI exposes the independent controls only when the effective revision action is granted, clearly explains the period rule, preserves original/revised display and shows immutable approval history. Hidden controls do not replace server authorization.
- Additive migration `20261004144941_V39FieldSpecificKpiRevisions` introduces only bounded nullable revised values and false-default flags; it does not manufacture revision history or overwrite administrator data.
- Relational tests prove independent OPMS/IPMS field persistence, filtered immutable history, stale-writer rejection and early/late resolution. Component tests prove the independently flagged payload, action-driven UI and history experience.

With full-suite verification recorded below, R-22 and assessment area 10 are **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Native SQL Server application and concurrency evidence remains explicitly isolated under area 31 rather than inferred from SQLite.

The complete suites pass **330 backend tests**, with one environment-gated SQL Server test skipped, and **158/158 frontend tests across 50 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, the Release/backend and production/frontend builds, bundle budget, model consistency and idempotent SQL generation pass.

### 11.83 End-to-end HTTP tenant-isolation matrix

The missing single-scenario repository proof now runs through the real ASP.NET Core test host rather than invoking controllers directly:

- A disposable relational SQLite database creates two municipalities, their departments, users, targets, submissions, canonical period targets, POE metadata and audit rows. The Municipality A operator, tenant role and permissions are created dynamically in the database.
- The authenticated request traverses CSRF, authentication, tenant resolution, dynamic permission evaluation, global EF query filters, controllers, formatters and idempotency. The test proves Municipality A's target register excludes Municipality B, direct target fetch returns not found, and direct submission edit and approval attempts cannot resolve the Municipality B record.
- Multipart POE upload and protected content-download attempts against Municipality B return not found before storage access. The performance summary contains exactly Municipality A's target, submission and department, and the paged audit response contains Municipality A evidence without Municipality B evidence.
- A second HTTP test supplies Municipality B through `X-Municipality-Id`; tenant resolution rejects the forged selection with 403 `TENANT_CONTEXT_DENIED` before controller execution.
- `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Testing` hosts the actual application entry point. Test-only authentication changes no production authentication path, the database and evidence directory are unique per test and removed after connection-pool release, and all application queries remain provider-neutral for SQL Server deployment.

The focused HTTP suite passes **2/2** and the complete backend suite passes **332 tests**, with one environment-gated native SQL Server test skipped. R-02 and final assessment area 2 are now **COMPLIANT** at repository level. Native SQL Server execution remains separately classified as Not Verifiable under area 31, and deployed penetration/UAT evidence remains under areas 4 and 32.

### 11.84 Required official report-family breadth

The synchronous official-report pipeline now covers every report class explicitly required by V3.9 rather than applying a performance-row renderer to every output:

- A governed `OfficialReportType` catalogue defines Quarterly, Mid-Term, Annual, Departmental, Unit, Performance Summary, Workflow Status, Submission, Verification, Approval, PMS, Internal Audit, Outstanding RFI, POE/Evidence, Audit Trail and Version Trail reports. Each class has an allow-listed column contract and class-specific headings; a template lineage cannot change class.
- Generation resolves class-specific authoritative data from canonical period targets/submissions, workflow instances and action ledgers, Internal Audit assessments, RFIs, evidence metadata, append-only audit trails and KPI field/period revision history. Department and unit reports require valid tenant-owned filters, while Quarterly/Mid-Term/Annual classes require the corresponding governed period type.
- Report generation and history require the intersection of dynamic report permission, KPI READ scope and submission READ scope. Audit-trail output additionally requires `Audit.Trails.View`. Downloads re-evaluate those capabilities and the stored authorization scope, so hiding a menu or button is not the security boundary.
- The SPA exposes all 16 classes in approved-template administration, preserves class identity across immutable template versions, prompts for required department/unit filters, labels generation history by report class and preserves stored filters during regeneration.
- Migration `20261004161339_V39OfficialReportFamilies` adds constrained report-class identity to templates and generations, backfilling existing performance outputs as Quarterly without rewriting historic files or hashes.
- Automated evidence enumerates all 16 layouts, rejects cross-class columns, proves deterministic class-specific rendering and proves direct generation denial when underlying resource READ permission is absent. The complete suites pass **335 backend tests** with one explicitly environment-gated SQL Server test skipped and **158/158 frontend tests**.

This closes the analytical/oversight/evidence report-family breadth portion of R-41. Section 11.85 subsequently closes durable asynchronous generation and governed schedule/distribution. Area 20 remains compliant because official output, immutable versioning, scope re-authorization and every required report class are repository-complete; native SQL Server and deployment acceptance remain separately tracked.

### 11.85 Durable asynchronous report generation, scheduling and distribution

The remaining repository-controlled R-41 scheduling and distribution gap is closed:

- `OfficialReportJob` is a durable municipality-owned queue record with exact template/FY/period/filter inputs, retry and processing state, attempt/timing/error evidence, requester identity, recipient/channel snapshot, immutable result-generation link and SQL Server `rowversion`. Stale processing work is recovered and transient failures use bounded retry; authorization or validation failures terminate without generating output.
- `OfficialReportSchedule` is an immutable version lineage with Once/Daily/Weekly/Monthly cadence, interval, next occurrence, effective bounds, active state, approval reference, reason, department/unit filters, dynamic user/role recipients and IN_APP/EMAIL/SMS channels. Editing appends a successor instead of rewriting history, and schedule occurrence uniqueness prevents duplicate materialization after downtime or concurrent polling.
- The hosted worker calls the same secured official-generation action used by direct requests, under the persisted requester and municipality context. Report GENERATE, KPI READ, submission READ, record scope and Audit Trail permission are therefore re-evaluated at execution; a hidden UI action cannot bypass the API, and cross-municipality data cannot be selected.
- Successful jobs link the new immutable `OfficialReportGeneration`, create `ReportReady` in-app notifications, and enqueue the existing governed delivery outbox with recipient/channel snapshots. The outbox link supplies retry and receipt evidence; schedules with no effective recipients fail closed.
- `/api/v1/reports/official/jobs` and `/schedules` expose bounded job history, queue, retry, versioned schedule save and run-now operations with dynamic permission checks, exact tenant-owned foreign-key validation, reasons, audit and RowVersion conflict handling. The Reports SPA exposes immediate queueing, generation-version queueing, job state/error/delivery history, schedule versioning and failed-job retry without treating button visibility as authorization.
- Migration `20261004170828_V39OfficialReportJobsAndDistribution` adds only the two governed tables, restricted tenant/template/year/period/filter/generation/outbox relationships, cadence/state/check constraints, version/occurrence uniqueness and native SQL Server rowversions. Four relational tests prove successful scheduled generation and distribution, zero-recipient fail-closed behavior, direct queue denial without underlying resource permission, and RowVersion-protected schedule succession.

**Status:** R-41 is **COMPLIANT at repository level**. Production provider delivery, representative-volume performance and native SQL Server execution remain deployment acceptance evidence rather than missing repository functionality.

### 11.86 Municipality/year-configurable SDBIP layers

The previously missing SDBIP-layer foundation is now implemented without introducing a provider-specific application path:

- `SdbipLayer` is a tenant-owned, financial-year-specific controlled master with stable public identity, normalized code, administrator-defined display name and order, soft-active state, audit timestamps and RowVersion. Composite foreign keys prevent a layer from referencing another municipality's year and prevent an OPMS KPI from referencing another municipality's layer.
- Versioned master endpoints and the Tenant Calendar administration surface support create, read and reasoned update/deactivation under dynamic `SDBIP_LAYER` CRUD permissions. Duplicate codes are rejected per municipality financial year, stale updates conflict, and a layer with active non-withdrawn KPIs cannot be deactivated.
- OPMS create and update resolve the selected layer by public ID, require it to be active and to belong to the exact municipality financial year selected through the canonical period plan, and return layer identity in the public response. The target form loads active persisted layers and filters them by the selected fiscal year; direct API calls retain the same enforcement.
- Migration `20261004175415_V39GovernedSdbipLayers` creates the governed master, composite alternate/foreign keys, uniqueness and ordering indexes, native SQL Server RowVersion, and a nullable legacy-cutover `OpmsTargets.SdbipLayerId`. New writes require the relationship; historic rows remain nullable until controlled reconciliation so the migration does not invent layer ownership.

Two focused relational tests prove audited administration, tenant filtering, duplicate prevention, composite cross-tenant rejection, portable stale-writer behavior and protected deactivation. Existing normalized-target tests now prove persistence through the required layer relationship, while the administration test proves production API transport. The complete suites pass **341 backend tests** with one explicitly environment-gated SQL Server test skipped and **158/158 frontend tests across 50 files**.

This closes the repository-controlled configurable-layer and exact-year target-association prerequisite. Area 18 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** because bulk OPMS/SDBIP import, row-level validation/reconciliation and historic target-to-layer assignment are separate open work.

## SQL Server Revalidation Required

SQLite remains an interim development and relational-test provider. Before production readiness is claimed, execute and retain evidence for the following against a positively identified SQL Server/Azure SQL environment:

- clean and upgrade application of every committed migration, including `20261002204550_V39CanonicalSubmissionBaseState`, its legacy-state backfill, `20261003145227_V39InternalAuditAssessmentModels`, `20261003152131_V39OfficialReportGeneration`, `20261003160701_V39NotificationPoliciesAndScheduling`, `20261003161402_V39NotificationPreferences`, `20261003170339_V39CanonicalActualPerformanceCutover`, `20261003230747_V39TenantScopedPagedAudit`, `20261004031802_V39PeriodBasedKpiOrdering`, `20261004144941_V39FieldSpecificKpiRevisions`, `20261004161339_V39OfficialReportFamilies`, `20261004170828_V39OfficialReportJobsAndDistribution` and `20261004175415_V39GovernedSdbipLayers`;
- native generated `rowversion`, stale-writer conflicts and all filtered/unique/check indexes;
- decimal precision, UTC/date behavior, restricted cascades and workflow/audit transaction rollback;
- tenant/security query plans, bounded register load behavior, locking and concurrency under representative volume;
- security persistence, permission revocation, direct HTTP bypass attempts and delegated-administrator privilege-escalation tests;
- backup, checksum, isolated restore, integrity verification, measured RPO/RTO and failover rehearsal.

Successful SQLite tests do not certify these SQL Server behaviors. Provider cutover remains configuration plus migrations; no SQLite-specific domain model or authorization path was introduced.

## 12. Final independent V3.9 post-implementation verification — 2026-10-03

This section is the final independent verification against the complete authoritative `OPMS Requirements V3.9.docx` supplied with the repository (1,144 paragraphs and 133 tables were extracted and reviewed). It supersedes the status counts and any repository-complete claims in earlier sections where they conflict. Earlier sections remain as implementation history and test chronology; they are not the final acceptance decision.

### 11.87 Governed OPMS/SDBIP import and reconciliation

Status: **CLOSED AT REPOSITORY LEVEL — native SQL Server acceptance remains**

- Added tenant- and SDBIP-layer-scoped staged import batches with unique client request IDs, source hashes, immutable row evidence and RowVersion-protected commit.
- Reconciliation classifies each spreadsheet row as `NEW`, `UNCHANGED`, `CHANGED` or `INVALID`; diagnostics retain source row, KPI reference, reporting period, field, supplied value, code and reason.
- The wide CSV parser maps Q1, Q2, Mid-Term, Q3, Q4 and Annual target/unit/budget columns into canonical period rows and rejects unknown units instead of silently defaulting them.
- Business department/unit codes are resolved inside the active tenant; duplicate KPI references, cross-layer/year ambiguity, unreconciled legacy periods and attempts to rewrite original fields fail during preview.
- Commit is all-or-nothing. New rows create canonical `PerformancePeriodTarget` records; changed rows require reason, approval reference and effective date, preserve originals, and append KPI-field and Q3/Q4/Annual target/budget revision history.
- The protected API and navigation require dynamic `OPMS_KPI.IMPORT` / `NAV.SDBIP.IMPORT` permission, so hiding the UI does not authorize direct calls.
- Added separately authorized, scope-filtered and audited downloads for a blank governed template and a current-layer export. Exported period/unit/direction values round-trip through the same parser, spreadsheet formula prefixes are neutralized and revised values resolve only in their applicable periods.
- Remaining acceptance: execute the migration/import constraint and concurrency matrix on native SQL Server.

- Relational coverage proves page size/metadata, search and tenant exclusion, while frontend type-check, component tests and production build cover the migrated consumers.

### 11.100 Public-ID organization scope contracts and final selector cutover

Status: **CLOSED AT REPOSITORY LEVEL FOR PRODUCTION CONSUMERS — compatibility routes are retired in section 11.127; native SQL Server query-plan acceptance remains**

- Removed the final production calls to the legacy full-array department and unit client functions. OPMS/IPMS capture, OPMS register filtering, security role assignment and permission simulation use the bounded `OrganizationMasterPicker`; section 11.127 records the separately governed retirement of the unused compatibility definitions and routes.
- OPMS register department and lifecycle filters now execute in the authorized database query before `Count` and paging. The SPA no longer filters the current page locally or derives totals from a truncated result, and the server rejects unsupported lifecycle values.
- OPMS/IPMS create and update requests now carry stable department/unit public IDs. A shared resolver validates active records within the selected municipality, derives a department from a selected unit where appropriate, rejects cross-tenant and mismatched relationships, and persists the existing numeric foreign keys internally. Responses project the stable public IDs back to the client, so no provider-specific storage change is required.
- Dynamic security role assignments now accept and return stable department/unit public IDs and labels while retaining numeric fields only for backward-compatible callers. Resolution is tenant-scoped and active-state checked before persistence; unit/department mismatches and cross-municipality values fail closed. ### 11.152 Effective-dated operational assignment enforcement

**Requirement:** R-08 dynamic record/scope security and effective-dated assignments must deny inactive, future and expired responsibility grants on direct API queries as well as individual record checks.

- `AccessControlService.GetEffectiveAccessAsync` now selects only active operational assignments whose optional start is not in the future and whose optional end is still strictly current. Expired, future and disabled assignments never enter the effective-access result used by record decisions, access simulation or effective-permission views.
- Because `GetQueryScopeAsync` consumes that same effective result, an expired or future target/KPI assignment can no longer contribute a database-list filter even though the individual-record matcher would later reject it. This aligns list and detail authorization and removes a potential inference path.

### 11.154 Append-preserved operational-assignment administration and non-destructive controlled seeding

Status: **CLOSED AT REPOSITORY LEVEL FOR USER SCOPE/ASSIGNMENT HISTORY PRESERVATION — native SQL Server execution remains**

- Replaced the legacy operational-assignment hard-delete/recreate mutation with an effective-dated successor operation. Existing active assignments are closed in place, historic rows remain queryable, and the persistence boundary now rejects hard deletion of both user-scope and operational-assignment history.
- Assignment changes require an explicit 5–500 character reason and the target user's current RowVersion. The target user aggregate is advanced in the same transaction, so a stale editor receives HTTP 409 and cannot overwrite a concurrent assignment change. User responses now expose the required concurrency token.
- The mutation validates assignment-specific record selectors, date intervals, duplicates, self-delegation and active delegators. Delegators are resolved through the selected municipality before any write, so a Municipality B identity cannot be used to grant Municipality A responsibility.
- Controlled administrator/demo seeding now uses initial-only helpers: once any administrator-managed scope or assignment exists for a seeded user, startup leaves that configuration unchanged. Repeated execution is idempotent and cannot silently replace security configuration.
- Relational SQLite coverage proves non-destructive repeated seed execution, persistence-level hard-delete rejection, historic assignment closure, successor creation, audit-reason persistence, RowVersion advancement, stale-write rejection and cross-tenant delegator denial. ### 11.173 Enterprise-authentication sensitive-member enforcement

**Requirement:** R-08 database-driven member permissions must protect sensitive fields throughout direct API reads and writes, prevent inference through search and sort, and drive the production UI without requiring recompilation.

**Status:** Enterprise identity and authentication-event administration now apply the same central member-security boundary already used by user, employee and submission administration. This closes the confirmed authentication-secondary-resource gap; R-08 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** while the remaining sensitive secondary-resource catalogue and deployed HTTP privilege-escalation acceptance are incomplete.

**Implementation and evidence:**

- `AUTHENTICATION` is now explicitly field-security capable. Its controlled additive registry contains six sensitive members: linked-user email, expected enterprise email, issuer, external subject, event user identity and event IP address. The seeder creates nine read/update permissions, remains idempotent, and maps existing `AUTHENTICATION.LINK_IDENTITIES` and `AUTHENTICATION.VIEW_EVENTS` role grants to the corresponding member permissions without overwriting administrator choices.
- Authenticator pages independently evaluate each read grant, redact denied DTO members, exclude denied columns from search predicates and reject email sorting when the expected-email member is denied. Authentication-event pages likewise redact user/IP values and exclude them from search. Direct identity provisioning requires `ExpectedEmail.UPDATE` and additionally requires issuer/subject update grants when those optional fields are supplied.
- The production authentication-administration UI derives email, event-user and IP visibility from effective member permissions, avoids the protected email sort when denied, hides protected columns and prevents identity provisioning without the expected-email update grant. The server remains authoritative when UI controls are hidden.
- Relational tests prove redaction, denied sensitive search, denied sensitive sort and direct-write denial. Catalogue tests prove all six definitions, their sensitivity flags, field-security capability and nine permission definitions. Component tests prove permission-driven columns, provisioning controls and the non-sensitive fallback sort.
- Local verification passed **463 backend tests** with one explicit native SQL Server-environment skip and **258/258 frontend tests across 65 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, Release build and SQL Server-provider model/snapshot consistency also passed. Exact clean-runner verification for commit `a923a53` is green in GitHub Actions run `37548803777` in 4m06s, including migration/recovery validation, accessibility automation, production build, bundle-budget enforcement, dependency audits and secret scan.

### 11.184 OPMS/IPMS official-report schedule and durable-job member security

**Requirement:** R-08 database-driven member permissions must independently protect recipient and operator identities plus operational diagnostics in official-report schedule and durable-job responses, prevent denied requester/error inference through search, retain record scope and drive production UI visibility without recompilation.

**Status:** Both official-report resources now apply fail-closed, record-scoped member security to schedule recipients/creator identity and durable-job requester/error/distribution/recipient/retry metadata. This closes the confirmed schedule/job operational-metadata disclosure and search-inference gap; R-08 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending the residual sensitive-secondary-resource audit and deployed HTTP privilege-escalation acceptance.

**Implementation and evidence:**

- The additive registry defines seven sensitive, system-managed members under each of `OPMS_REPORT` and `IPMS_REPORT`: `ScheduleRecipientValues`, `ScheduleCreatedBy`, `JobRequestedBy`, `JobLastError`, `JobDistributionOutboxPublicId`, `JobRecipientUserIds` and `JobRetryReason`. Corresponding read permissions are generated idempotently and added to the existing configuration-administrator compatibility mapping without replacing administrator-authored rules; ordinary `Reports.View` remains insufficient.
- Bounded schedule/job pages and create, queue, run-now and retry mutation responses evaluate each member through the central municipality/department/unit/owner scope. Denied strings and receipt identities serialize as `null`, denied recipient collections serialize empty, and permission decisions are cached only for identical row scopes within a page.
- Job search always covers non-sensitive template/year/period fields, but requester name and last-error predicates are included only where the respective member query scope permits the row. This prevents denied values from leaking through result counts while retaining municipality, department, unit and self-scope semantics.
- The production reports workspace independently gates all seven sensitive values, including hostile payloads. Requester/error/distribution/recipients/retry detail and schedule recipients/creator become visible immediately when their exact member grants enter the effective permission set; schedule version selection cannot copy denied recipient values into the editing form.
- Relational tests prove default page and mutation-response masking, zero requester/error search matches under denial, scoped immediate visibility after grant and complete registry metadata for both report resources. Component coverage proves denied hostile values remain absent and all granted job/schedule values render without recompilation.
- Local verification passed **471 backend tests** with one explicit native SQL Server-environment skip and **269/269 frontend tests across 65 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, Release build, Vite production build, **2/2** accessibility tests, recovery-script validation, diff hygiene, SQL Server-provider model/snapshot consistency and the **78-chunk** bundle budget with a **401.3 KiB** largest chunk passed. Exact clean-runner verification for commit `9bee8cd` is green in GitHub Actions run `37569206924` in 5m10s, including migration/recovery validation, dependency audits and secret scan.

### 11.185 OPMS/IPMS official-report generation metadata security

**Requirement:** R-08 database-driven member permissions must independently protect immutable official-generation authorization/filter snapshots, data-version references and generator identity, prevent denied generator-search inference, preserve stored record scope and keep regeneration safe without consuming hidden raw metadata.

**Status:** Both official-report resources now apply fail-closed, stored-scope member security to the complete confirmed sensitive generation metadata surface. This closes the confirmed generation-history disclosure and search-inference gap; R-08 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending the residual sensitive-secondary-resource audit and deployed HTTP privilege-escalation acceptance.

**Implementation and evidence:**

- The additive registry now defines eleven sensitive, system-managed members under each of `OPMS_REPORT` and `IPMS_REPORT`: the seven schedule/job members in section 11.184 plus `GenerationScopeJson`, `GenerationFilterJson`, `GenerationDataVersionReference` and `GenerationGeneratedBy`. Corresponding read permissions are generated idempotently and added to the existing configuration-administrator compatibility mapping without replacing administrator-authored rules.
- Bounded generation history and immediate generation mutation responses evaluate each protected value against the immutable normalized scope stored with that generation. Missing permission, invalid/legacy scope schema, unrestricted stored data presented to a scoped member grant, or any unmatched department/unit/owner/target grant fails closed and serializes the protected member as `null`.
- Generator username/email search predicates are admitted only for generation rows allowed by `GenerationGeneratedBy.READ`; denied identities cannot change result counts. Non-sensitive template, year, period and file searches remain available.
- Safe typed department/unit public identifiers are returned separately for authorized report regeneration. The production UI no longer parses or trusts raw `FilterJson`; hostile hidden filter content cannot redirect a regenerate/queue request. Generator identity and data-version hashes render only when their exact dynamic member grants are effective.
- Relational coverage proves base stored-scope paging, four-field default masking, typed safe-filter preservation, zero denied generator-search matches and immediate scoped visibility of all four fields after database permission change. Component coverage proves hostile raw scope/filter/actor/hash payloads remain absent under denial, typed selections drive regeneration and newly granted actor/hash members appear without recompilation.
- Local verification passed **471 backend tests** with one explicit native SQL Server-environment skip and **270/270 frontend tests across 65 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, Release build, Vite production build, **2/2** accessibility tests, recovery-script validation, diff hygiene, SQL Server-provider model/snapshot consistency and the **78-chunk** bundle budget with a **401.4 KiB** largest chunk passed. ### 11.186 Notification-policy member and action security

**Requirement:** R-08 database-driven security must independently protect notification recipient definitions and message templates, govern policy lifecycle and execution actions, retain tenant scope and enforce the same decisions on direct API calls and production controls without recompilation.

**Status:** Notification-policy reads and writes now use a dedicated dynamic resource, three member permissions and six action permissions in addition to the existing coarse workflow-configuration boundary. This closes the confirmed notification-policy template/recipient disclosure and action-bypass gap; R-08 remains **PARTIALLY COMPLIANT** pending the residual sensitive-secondary-resource audit and deployed HTTP privilege-escalation acceptance.

**Implementation and evidence:**

- The additive registry defines `NOTIFICATION_POLICY` with dynamic CRUD and record criteria, sensitive editable `RecipientValues`, `TitleTemplate` and `MessageTemplate` members, and independent `ACTIVATE`, `COPY`, `SET_DELIVERY_STATE`, `PREVIEW`, `TEST` and `RUN_DUE` actions. The existing configuration-administrator compatibility mapping receives these grants idempotently without overwriting administrator-authored rules.
- The bounded policy page requires dynamic READ and masks denied templates as `null` and denied recipient arrays as empty. Draft creation requires dynamic CREATE plus UPDATE for all three protected members; direct activate, copy, pause/resume, preview, test and due-run calls require their exact action grants. Preview/test additionally require template READ because their responses reveal rendered content.
- Every decision is evaluated in the authenticated municipality scope through the central access-control service. Removing resource, member or action permission therefore affects the next request without recompilation, while the existing policy boundary remains defense in depth.
- The production administration workspace fetches, renders, edits and exposes actions only under the same resource/member/action capabilities. Copy additionally requires CREATE and all protected-member UPDATE grants, matching the server contract and preventing a visible action that must fail.
- Relational tests prove default template/recipient masking, immediate visibility after member grants, resource-read revocation, protected-write denial and preview-action revocation/regrant. Registry coverage proves all three sensitive editable members and all six actions. A hostile-payload component test proves denied recipients/templates/actions remain absent or disabled and appear immediately when the effective permission set changes.
- Local verification passed **471 backend tests** with one explicit native SQL Server-environment skip and **271/271 frontend tests across 65 files**. TypeScript, ESLint, Release build, Vite production build, **2/2** accessibility tests, recovery-script validation, diff hygiene, SQL Server-provider model/snapshot consistency and the **78-chunk** bundle budget with a **401.4 KiB** largest chunk passed. Exact clean-runner verification for commit `cdfebed` is green in GitHub Actions run `37572774122` in 3m49s, including migration/recovery validation, dependency audits and secret scan.

### 12.1 Final verdict

**NOT FULLY COMPLIANT — NOT PRODUCTION READY.**

The repository contains substantial, tested implementation for tenant-aware security, configurable workflows, governed submissions, POE handling, global National KPA/Back-to-Basics references, municipality/year-scoped strategic planning masters, canonical seven-reference OPMS/IPMS KPI classification, governed KPI/indicator/functional/standard/unit classifications, optional hierarchy relationships, governed budget types and multi-source budget allocations, effective-dated strategic risks and KPI relationships, TID, Circular 88, IDP, audit, notification operations and bounded registers. All executable repository quality gates available in this environment pass. Those facts do not close the remaining V3.9 product gaps or the deployment evidence gaps. Fixture-backed generic lookup routes have been retired or mapped to authoritative administration, and new OPMS/IPMS target writes use normalized period records plus governed strategic, performance-classification, display-unit and budget foreign keys with controlled historic reconciliation. Mid-Term/Annual suggestions now have central calculation, separate suggested/final persistence, immutable history, protected APIs and a production SPA workflow. Both configurable Internal Audit models now have governed tenant/year selection, model-specific capture, RFI/rating integration and immutable reassessment history. Official reports now cover all 16 required classes through versioned approved templates, immutable stored CSV/XLSX/DOCX/PDF output, durable asynchronous jobs, versioned schedules, dynamic recipients, delivery evidence, snapshot hashes and execution/download re-authorization. Notification policies now provide versioned inheritance, working-calendar reminders/escalations, downtime catch-up, SMS, persisted preferences and terminal missing-address handling. Native SQL Server plus operational acceptance has not been executed here.

### 12.4 Remediation completed during this final pass

- Added an explicit relational dynamic-security integration test for two runtime-created roles, navigation visibility, CRUD permission and department scope.
- Changed the existing dynamic-security tests from EF InMemory to relational SQLite so foreign keys and relational behavior participate in the checks.
- Added the required canonical `baseState` to all frontend OPMS/IPMS submission fixtures, fixing the type-check failure revealed by the final gate.
- Added the authorized performance-catalogue API and replaced target-form and target-library fixture dependencies with persisted catalogue, organization, employee and related-target API data. Secondary generic lookup prototypes remain explicitly tracked under area 26.
- Cut new OPMS/IPMS target writes over to normalized `PerformancePeriodTarget` records. Missing municipality-year/period masters fail closed, unit values are centrally normalized, general edits may add missing backfill rows but cannot alter/remove governed values, and legacy-shaped responses are read-only projections of normalized rows.
- Added `OPMS_KPI.NORMALIZE_LEGACY` and a bounded, tenant-scoped target-normalization preview/execute API. It requires explicit target public IDs and a reason, attributes created rows to the authenticated operator, audits successful reconciliation and refuses ambiguous legacy revised values rather than manufacturing missing approval history.

### 12.6 Migration safety conclusion

The current EF model matches the committed snapshot, the canonical submission-state migration includes an explicit legacy backfill, the Internal Audit migration contains only its two governed tables, the original official-report migration contains only its template/generation tables and constraints/indexes, the report-family migration adds only the two class columns plus bounded constraints, the asynchronous-report migration contains only its governed schedule/job tables and relationships, the SDBIP-layer migration is additive while preserving nullable historic target rows for controlled reconciliation, the global-reference migration contains only its two masters and two availability mappings, the strategic-planning migration contains only its five masters and five optional relationship tables, the KPI-classification migration adds fourteen nullable references with deterministic unique-match backfill before restricted FK creation, the governed-budget migration adds four masters/junction tables plus two nullable target references with conservative referenced-value backfill before restricted target FK creation, the governed-performance-classification migration adds four masters plus seven nullable target references with per-tenant distinct-value backfill before restricted target FK creation, the KPI-unit migration adds one master plus two nullable target references with a preservation-oriented per-municipality copy/backfill before restricted composite target FKs, the Vote Number year-scope migration adds one nullable historical key with conservative linked-KPI/single-year reconciliation before its composite tenant/year FK and filtered unique index, the canonical OPMS Unit/Performance Direction migration adds two exact seeded masters plus three nullable historical relationships with deterministic known-enum backfill before restricted foreign keys, the target-template identity migration additively backfills four unique public identifiers before enforcing them, introduces SQL Server native `rowversion` concurrency and enforces one logical version number per template, the security-history identity migration additively backfills four unique public identifiers before enforcing them while adding native SQL Server `rowversion` to mutable scope and assignment histories, and the IDP secondary-identity migration additively backfills eleven unique public identifiers before enforcing them while adding native SQL Server `rowversion` to eight mutable secondary records. Ambiguous or absent legacy classifications, Vote Number years and unknown unit/direction values are deliberately not fabricated. Relational SQLite tests exercise the shared model, and idempotent SQL Server scripts can be generated. These are useful pre-deployment controls, but they do not prove production migration safety. The historical chain must still be applied both to a clean SQL Server database and to a production-shaped upgrade copy, followed by reconciliation of row counts/ownership/state, constraint and filtered-index inspection, `rowversion` concurrency tests, rollback/transaction checks and backup/restore validation. Until that evidence exists, SQL Server migration safety remains **Not Verifiable** and production deployment must not be approved.

### 12.7 Release decision

The completed remediation improves correctness and closes several repository-level gaps, including all required official report classes, durable generation/scheduling/distribution, version history and bounded production target selection, but it does not make the product fully compliant. Release approval still requires execution/sign-off of governed historical target reconciliation and physical legacy-column retirement, plus successful native SQL Server, security, performance and operational acceptance. The explicit final verdict remains **NOT FULLY COMPLIANT — NOT PRODUCTION READY**.
